Example of Figure 3.2 . 4. Fully discrete schemes In this section we turn to the study of some temporal discretizations of the o.d.e. systems
represented by the standard Galerkin spatial discretizations of $(\ref{cb})$ or $(\ref{scb})$ ,
such as $(\ref{eq22})$ or $(\ref{eq24})$ , for example. We shall confine ourselves to explicit time stepping schemes in order to avoid the more costly implicit methods
that require solving nonlinear systems of equations at each time step. Of course, with
explicit methods there arises the issue of stability of the fully discrete schemes.
We will not be exhaustive in our analysis but we will study as examples three simple,
well known explicit Runge-Kutta temporal discretizations, that require, respectively,
stability conditions of the type $k=O(h^{2})$ , $k=O(h^{4/3})$ , and $k\leq\lambda_{0}h$ for $\lambda_{0}$ sufficiently small, where $k$ is the time step. 4.1. The explicit Euler scheme Let $M$ be a positive integer, $k=T/M$ denote the (uniform) time step, and put $t^{n}=nk$ , $n=0,1,\dots,M$ . We consider the standard Galerkin semidiscretizations with piecewise linear,
continuous functions on a uniform spatial mesh on $[0,1]$ with $h=1/N$ , given by the
initial-value problems $(\ref{eq27})$ , $(\ref{eq220})$ and $(\ref{eq29})$ , $(\ref{eq220})$ in the case
of the $(\ref{cb})$ and the $(\ref{scb})$ systems, respectively. We discretize the systems in time
with the explicit Euler scheme. Hence, we seek for $0\leq n\leq M$ $H_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{2}$ , $U_{h}^{n}\in S_{h,0}^{2}$ , approximations of the solution $\eta(x,t^{n})$ , $u(x,t^{n})$ of the $(\ref{cb})$ system, such that for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ $\displaystyle(H_{h}^{n+1}-H_{h}^{n},\phi)$ $\displaystyle+k(U_{hx}^{n},\phi)+k((H_{h}^{n}U_{h}^{n})_{x}),\phi)=0\quad\forall\phi\in S_{h}^{2}\,,$ (4.1) $\displaystyle a(U_{h}^{n+1}-U_{h}^{n},\chi)$ $\displaystyle+k(H_{hx}^{n},\chi)+k(U_{h}^{n}U_{hx}^{n},\chi)=0\quad\forall\chi\in S_{h,0}^{2}\,,$ with $H_{h}^{0}=I_{h}\eta^{0}\,,\quad U_{h}^{0}=I_{h,0}u^{0}\,.$ (4.2) The analogous fully discrete approximation of the $(\ref{scb})$ system is defined, for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ , by $\displaystyle(H_{h}^{n+1}-H_{h}^{n},\phi)$ $\displaystyle+k(U_{hx}^{n},\phi)+\tfrac{k}{2}((H_{h}^{n}U_{h}^{n})_{x}),\phi)=0\quad\forall\phi\in S_{h}^{2}\,,$ (4.3) $\displaystyle a(U_{h}^{n+1}-U_{h}^{n},\chi)$ $\displaystyle+k(H_{hx}^{n},\chi)+\tfrac{3k}{2}(U_{h}^{n}U_{hx}^{n},\chi)+\tfrac{k}{2}(H_{h}^{n}H_{hx}^{n},\chi)=0\quad\forall\chi\in S_{h,0}^{2}\,,$ with $H_{h}^{0}=I_{h}\eta^{0}\,,\quad U_{h}^{0}=I_{h,0}u^{0}\,.$ (4.4) Let $A:L^{2}\to S_{h,0}^{2}$ , be defined for $f\in L^{2}$ by $a(Af,\chi)=(f,\chi)\quad\forall\chi\in S_{h,0}^{2}\,,$ (4.5) i.e. as the discrete solution operator such that $w_{h}=Af$ , where $w_{h}$ is the standard Galerkin
approximation in $S_{h,0}^{2}$ of the solution of the two-point bvp $-\tfrac{1}{3}w^{\prime\prime}+w=f$ , $0\leq x\leq 1$ , $w(0)=w(1)=0$ . From $(\ref{eq45})$ we have immediately that $\|Af\|_{1}\leq C\|f\|_{-1}\,,$ (4.6) where the $\|\cdot\|_{-1}$ norm is defined for $f\in L^{2}$ by $\|f\|_{-1}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in H_{0}^{1}\\
g\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{(f,g)}{\|g\|_{1}}\,.$ With this notation in place and letting as before $P$ denote the $L^{2}$ projection operator
onto $S_{h}^{2}$ , we may rewrite the fully discrete scheme $(\ref{eq41})$ , $(\ref{eq42})$ for $(\ref{cb})$ as $\displaystyle H_{h}^{n+1}-H_{h}^{n}$ $\displaystyle+kPU_{hx}^{n}+kP(H_{h}^{n}U_{h}^{n})_{x}=0\,,$ (4.7) $\displaystyle U_{h}^{n+1}-U_{h}^{n}$ $\displaystyle+kAH_{hx}^{n}+kA(U_{h}^{n}U_{hx}^{n})=0\,,$ for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ , with $H_{h}^{0}=I_{h}\eta^{0}$ , $U_{h}^{0}=I_{h,0}u^{0}$ .
Similarly, for $(\ref{scb})$ we have from $(\ref{eq43})$ , $(\ref{eq44})$ $\displaystyle H_{h}^{n+1}-H_{h}^{n}$ $\displaystyle+kPU_{hx}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}P(H_{h}^{n}U_{h}^{n})_{x}=0\,,$ (4.8) $\displaystyle U_{h}^{n+1}-U_{h}^{n}$ $\displaystyle+kAH_{hx}^{n}+\tfrac{3k}{2}A(U_{h}^{n}U_{hx}^{n})+\tfrac{k}{2}A(H_{h}^{n}H_{hx}^{n})=0\,,$ for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ , with $H_{h}^{0}=I_{h}\eta^{0}$ , $U_{h}^{0}=I_{h,0}u^{0}$ .
We will prove error estimates for the schemes $(\ref{eq47})$ and $(\ref{eq48})$ by comparing $H_{h}^{n}$ with $I_{h}\eta(t^{n})$ and $U_{h}^{n}$ with $I_{h,0}u(t^{n})$ .
For this purpose, it is useful to establish the following estimates of the truncation errors of the interpolants. (In the sequel we shall analyze mainly the
approximation of the $(\ref{scb})$ system; the analogous results for $(\ref{cb})$ follow as
in Sections $2$ and $3$ . Frequently, we shall suppress the $x$ variable, denoting e.g. $\eta(\cdot,t)$ by $\eta(t)$ etc.) Lemma 4.1 . Suppose that the solution $(\eta,u)$ of $(\ref{scb})$ is sufficiently
smooth in $[0,T]$ . Let $H(t)=I_{h}\eta(t)$ , $U(t)=I_{h,0}u(t)$ , and define $\psi(t)\in S_{h}^{2}$ , $\zeta(t)\in S_{h,0}^{2}$ for $0\leq t\leq T$ by $\displaystyle H_{t}+PU_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}P(HU)_{x}$ $\displaystyle=\psi\,,$ (4.9) $\displaystyle U_{t}+AH_{x}+\tfrac{3}{2}A(UU_{x})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(HH_{x})$ $\displaystyle=\zeta\,.$ (4.10) Then $\displaystyle\|\psi\|$ $\displaystyle\leq Ch^{3/2}\,,\quad\|\psi_{t}\|\leq Ch^{3/2}\,,$ (4.11) $\displaystyle\|\zeta\|_{1}$ $\displaystyle\leq Ch^{2}\,,\quad\,\,\,\,\|\zeta_{t}\|_{1}\leq Ch^{2}\,,$ hold for $0\leq t\leq T$ . An analogous result holds for $(\ref{cb})$ . Proof. Subtracting the equations $P(\eta_{t}+u_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}(u\eta)_{x})=0$ and $(\ref{eq49})$ , and putting $\rho:=\eta-I_{h}\eta$ , $\sigma:=u-I_{h,0}u$ , we
obtain $P\bigl(\rho_{t}+[(1+\tfrac{1}{2}\eta)\sigma]_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}(u\rho)_{x}-\tfrac{1}{2}(\rho\sigma)_{x}\bigr)=-\psi\,.$ Therefore, using the approximation properties of $S_{h}^{2}$ and $S_{h,0}^{2}$ , and
Lemma $2.2$ , we have $\|\psi\|\leq\|\rho_{t}\|+\|P[(1+\tfrac{1}{2}\eta)\sigma]_{x}\|+\tfrac{1}{2}\|P(u\rho)_{x}\|+\|(\rho\sigma)_{x}\|\leq C(h^{2}+h^{3/2}+h^{2}+h^{3})\leq Ch^{3/2}\,.$ Similarly, since e.g. by Lemma $2.2$ $\|P[(1+\tfrac{1}{2}\eta)\sigma]_{xt}\|\leq\|P(\tfrac{1}{2}\eta_{t}\sigma)_{x}\|+\|P[(1+\tfrac{1}{2}\eta)\sigma_{t}]_{x}\|\leq Ch^{3/2}\,,$ we have $\|\psi_{t}\|\leq\|\rho_{t}\|+\|P[(1+\tfrac{1}{2}\eta)\sigma]_{xt}\|+\tfrac{1}{2}\|P(u\rho)_{xt}\|+\|(\rho\sigma)_{xt}\|\leq C(h^{2}+h^{3/2}+h^{2}+h^{3})\leq Ch^{3/2}\,.$ Note now that for any $\chi\in S_{h,0}^{2}$ , $(\ref{eq45})$ and the fact that $(\sigma_{t}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})=0$ yield $a(A(u_{t}-\tfrac{1}{3}u_{txx})-U_{t},\chi)=(u_{t},\chi)+\tfrac{1}{3}(u_{tx},\chi_{x})-a(U_{t},\chi)=a(\sigma_{t},\chi)=(\sigma_{t},\chi)=a(A\sigma_{t},\chi)\,.$ Hence $A(u_{t}-\tfrac{1}{3}u_{txx})-U_{t}=A\sigma_{t}\,,$ which implies, in view of the second p.d.e. of $(\ref{scb})$ that $A\sigma_{t}+U_{t}+A(\eta_{x}+\tfrac{3}{2}uu_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}\eta\eta_{x})=0\,.$ Subtracting now this equation from $(\ref{eq410})$ we see, after some algebra, that $A\bigl(\sigma_{t}+\rho_{x}+\tfrac{3}{2}[(u\sigma)_{x}-\sigma\sigma_{x}]+\tfrac{1}{2}[(\eta\rho)_{x}-\rho\rho_{x}]\bigr)=-\zeta\,.$ (4.12) Therefore, using $(\ref{eq46})$ and the approximation properties of $S_{h}^{2}$ and $S_{h,0}^{2}$ , we obtain $\displaystyle\|\zeta\|_{1}$ $\displaystyle\leq C(\|\sigma_{t}\|_{-1}+\|\rho_{x}\|_{-1}+\|(u\sigma-\tfrac{1}{2}\sigma^{2})_{x}\|_{-1}+\|(\eta\rho-\tfrac{1}{2}\rho^{2})_{x}\|_{-1}$ $\displaystyle\leq C(\|\sigma_{t}\|+\|\rho\|+\|u\sigma\|+\|\sigma^{2}\|+\|\eta\rho\|+\|\rho^{2}\|)\leq Ch^{2}\,.$ Similarly, after differentiating $(\ref{eq412})$ with respect to $t$ , we see that $\|\zeta_{t}\|_{1}\leq Ch^{2}\,,$ thus ending the proof. The same results hold for $(\ref{cb})$ of course.
∎ We now proceed to prove error estimates for the explicit Euler-Galerkin schemes $(\ref{eq47})$ and $(\ref{eq48})$ . We begin by a consistency result. Lemma 4.2 . Suppose that the solution $(\eta,u)$ of $(\ref{scb})$ is sufficiently smooth.
Let $H^{n}:=H(t^{n})=I_{h}\eta(t^{n})$ , $U^{n}=U(t^{n})=I_{h,0}u(t^{n})$ , and define,
for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ , $\delta_{1}^{n}$ and $\delta_{2}^{n}$ by the equations $\displaystyle\delta_{1}^{n}$ $\displaystyle:=H^{n+1}-H^{n}+kPU_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}P(H^{n}U^{n})_{x}\,,$ $\displaystyle\delta_{2}^{n}$ $\displaystyle:=U^{n+1}-U^{n}+kAH_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{3k}{2}A(U^{n}U_{x}^{n})+\tfrac{k}{2}A(H^{n}H_{x}^{n})\,.$ Then $\max_{0\leq n\leq M-1}(\|\delta_{1}^{n}\|+\|\delta_{2}^{n}\|_{1})\leq Ck(k+h^{3/2})\,.$ The analogous result holds for $(\ref{cb})$ as well. Proof. Using $(\ref{eq49})$ and $(\ref{eq410})$ we have, with $\psi^{n}=\psi(t^{n})$ , $\zeta^{n}=\zeta(t^{n})$ , that $\delta_{1}^{n}=H^{n+1}-H^{n}-kH_{t}^{n}+k\psi^{n}$ , $\delta_{2}^{n}=U^{n+1}-U^{n}-kU_{t}^{n}+k\zeta^{n}$ . Hence, for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ , $\displaystyle\|\delta_{1}^{n}\|+\|\delta_{2}^{n}\|_{1}$ $\displaystyle\leq\|H^{n+1}-H^{n}-kH_{t}^{n}\|+k\|\psi^{n}\|+\|U^{n+1}-U^{n}-kU_{t}^{n}\|_{1}+k\|\zeta^{n}\|_{1}$ $\displaystyle\leq C(k^{2}+kh^{3/2}+k^{2}+kh^{2})\leq Ck(k+h^{3/2})\,,$ by Taylor’s theorem and $(\ref{eq411})$ .
∎ Proposition 4.1 . Suppose that the solutions $(\eta,u)$ of $(\ref{scb})$ and $(\ref{cb})$ are sufficiently smooth
on $[0,T]$ . Then, if $\mu=k/h^{2}$ , there is a constant $C=C(\mu)$ , which is an increasing
continuous function of $\mu$ , such that $\max_{0\leq n\leq M}\|H_{h}^{n}-\eta(t^{n})\|\leq C(k+h^{3/2})\,,\quad\max_{0\leq n\leq M}\|U_{h}^{n}-u(t^{n})\|_{1}\leq C(k+h)\,,$ (4.13) where $(H_{h}^{n},U_{h}^{n})$ satisfy $(\ref{eq47})$ or $(\ref{eq48})$ as the case may be. Proof. Consider the case of $(\ref{scb})$ . We use the notation of Lemmas $4.1$ and $4.2$ and put $\varepsilon^{n}=H^{n}-H_{h}^{n}$ , $e^{n}=U^{n}-U_{h}^{n}$ . Using the definition of $\delta_{1}^{n}$ , $\delta_{2}^{n}$ , and $(\ref{eq48})$ we obtain, after some straightforward
computations, that for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ $\displaystyle\varepsilon^{n+1}$ $\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{n}-kP[e_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}(H^{n}e^{n})_{x}-\tfrac{1}{2}(\varepsilon^{n}e^{n})_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}]+\delta_{1}^{n}\,,$ (4.14) $\displaystyle e^{n+1}$ $\displaystyle=e^{n}-kA[\varepsilon_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{3}{2}(U^{n}e^{n})_{x}-\tfrac{3}{2}(e^{n}e_{x}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}(H^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}-\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{n}\varepsilon_{x}^{n}]+\delta_{2}^{n}\,.$ (4.15) It follows from $(\ref{eq46})$ that $\displaystyle\|A(U^{n}e^{n})_{x}\|_{1}$ $\displaystyle\leq C\|U^{n}e^{n}\|\leq C\|e^{n}\|\,,$ (4.16) $\displaystyle\|A(H^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}\|_{1}$ $\displaystyle\leq C\|H^{n}\varepsilon^{n}\|\leq C\|\varepsilon^{n}\|\,.$ (4.17) Let now $0\leq n^{*}\leq M-1$ be the maximal integer such that $\|\varepsilon^{n}\|_{1}+\|e^{n}\|_{1}\leq 1\,,\quad 0\leq n\leq n^{*}\,.$ (4.18) Then, for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ , using $(\ref{eq46})$ , we have $\displaystyle\|P\bigl(e_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}(H^{n}e^{n})_{x}-\tfrac{1}{2}(\varepsilon^{n}e^{n})_{x}\bigr)\|$ $\displaystyle\leq\|e_{x}^{n}\|+\tfrac{1}{2}\|(H^{n}e^{n})_{x}\|+\tfrac{1}{2}\|(\varepsilon^{n}e^{n})_{x}\|$ (4.19) $\displaystyle\leq C(\|e^{n}\|_{1}+\|e^{n}\|_{1}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|_{1})\leq C\|e^{n}\|_{1}\,,$ $\|A(e^{n}e_{x}^{n})\|_{1}+\|A(\varepsilon^{n}\varepsilon_{x}^{n})\|_{1}\leq C(\|(e^{n})^{2}\|+\|(\varepsilon^{n})^{2}\|)\leq C(\|e^{n}\|+\|\varepsilon^{n}\|)\,.$ (4.20) Using $(\ref{eq416})$ - $(\ref{eq420})$ in $(\ref{eq414})$ and $(\ref{eq415})$ we have,
for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ $\displaystyle\|\varepsilon^{n+1}\|$ $\displaystyle\leq\|\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}P(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}\|+Ck\|e^{n}\|_{1}+\|\delta_{1}^{n}\|\,,$ (4.21) $\displaystyle\|e^{n+1}\|_{1}$ $\displaystyle\leq\|e^{n}\|_{1}+Ck(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|)+\|\delta_{2}^{n}\|_{1}\,.$ (4.22) Now $\|\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}P(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}\|^{2}=\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{4}\|P(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}\|^{2}-k(\varepsilon^{n},P(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x})\,.$ (4.23) But, by inverse assumptions $\|P(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}\|\leq\|(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}\|\leq\tfrac{C}{h}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|\,.$ In addition, $|(\varepsilon^{n},P(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x})|=|(\varepsilon^{n},(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x})|=\tfrac{1}{2}|(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\varepsilon^{n})|\leq C\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\,.$ Hence, $(\ref{eq423})$ yields $\|\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}P(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}\|^{2}\leq\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}+C\mu k\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}+Ck\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\,,$ i.e. that $\|\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}P(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}\|\leq(1+C_{\mu}k)\|\varepsilon^{n}\|\,,$ where $C_{\mu}$ is a generic polynomial in $\mu$ of degree one. Therefore $(\ref{eq421})$ becomes $\|\varepsilon^{n+1}\|\leq\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+C_{\mu}k(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})+\|\delta_{1}^{n}\|\,.$ (4.24) Hence, by $(\ref{eq422})$ , $(\ref{eq424})$ , and Lemma $4.2$ we obtain for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ $\|\varepsilon^{n+1}\|+\|e^{n+1}\|_{1}\leq\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1}+C_{\mu}k(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})+Ck(k+h^{3/2})\,.$ By Gronwall’s Lemma we conclude that $\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1}\leq C(\mu,T)(k+h^{3/2})\,,\quad 0\leq n\leq n^{*}+1\,,$ (4.25) where $C(\mu,T)=\exp((C_{0}+C_{1}\mu)T)$ . Taking $h$ sufficiently small, we see from the
maximality property $(\ref{eq418})$ of $n^{*}$ that we may take $n^{*}=M-1$ , and the conclusion
of the proposition follows from $(\ref{eq21})$ . The case of $(\ref{cb})$ is entirely similar.
∎ Remark 4.1 . The estimate $(\ref{eq425})$ and Sobolev’s inequality imply that $\|e^{n}\|_{\infty}=O(k+h^{3/2})$ . Therefore, $\max_{n}\|u(t^{n})-U_{h}^{n}\|_{\infty}=O(k+h^{3/2})$ . Remark 4.2 . Consider the linearized problem $(\ref{eq243})$ . In this case, the analogous
fully discrete scheme is $\displaystyle H_{h}^{n+1}-H_{h}^{n}+kPU_{hx}^{n}$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ $\displaystyle U_{h}^{n+1}-U_{h}^{n}+kAH_{hx}^{n}$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ , with $H_{h}^{0}=I_{h}\eta^{0}$ , $U_{h}^{0}=I_{h,0}u^{0}$ .
Consequently, using the notation of the proof of Proposition $4.1$ , we now have for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ the error equations $\displaystyle\varepsilon^{n+1}$ $\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{n}-kPe_{x}^{n}+\delta_{1}^{n}\,,$ $\displaystyle e^{n+1}$ $\displaystyle=e^{n}-kA\varepsilon_{x}^{n}+\delta_{2}^{n}\,,$ from which there easily follows the estimate $\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1}\leq C(k+h^{3/2})\,,\quad 0\leq n\leq M\,,$ and the conclusions of the analog of Proposition $4.1$ , without the stability restriction $k=O(h^{2})$ . In other words, the linearized system is not stiff . This may also be verified
by examining the spectrum of the spatial discretization operator of the semidiscrete linearized
system: The latter may be written for $0\leq t\leq T$ in the form $\displaystyle\eta_{ht}+L_{h}u_{h}$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ $\displaystyle M_{h}u_{ht}+\widetilde{L}_{h}\eta_{h}$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ where the operators $L_{h}:S_{h,0}^{2}\to S_{h}^{2}$ , $\widetilde{L}_{h}:S_{h}^{2}\to S_{h,0}^{2}$ , $M_{h}:S_{h,0}^{2}\to S_{h,0}^{2}$ are defined by the equations $\displaystyle(L_{h}\psi,\phi)$ $\displaystyle=(\psi_{x},\phi)\quad\forall\psi\in S_{h,0}^{2},\phi\in S_{h}^{2}\,,$ $\displaystyle(\widetilde{L}_{h}\phi,\psi)$ $\displaystyle=(\phi_{x},\psi)\quad\forall\phi\in S_{h}^{2},\psi\in S_{h,0}^{2}\,,$ $\displaystyle(M_{h}\psi,\chi)$ $\displaystyle=a(\psi,\chi)\quad\forall\psi,\chi\in S_{h,0}^{2}\,.$ Hence, the semidiscrete system may be written on $S_{h}^{2}\times S_{h,0}^{2}$ as $A_{h}W_{ht}+B_{h}W_{h}=0\,,$ where $W_{h}=[\eta_{h},u_{h}]^{T}$ , and $A_{h}=\begin{bmatrix}I&0\\
0&M_{h}\end{bmatrix}\,,\quad B_{h}=\begin{bmatrix}0&L_{h}\\
\widetilde{L}_{h}&0\end{bmatrix}\,.$ Therefore, the spectrum of the spatial discretization operator coincides with that of the
generalized eigenvalue problem $B_{h}V_{h}=-\lambda_{h}A_{h}V_{h}\,.$ (4.26) Denoting the eigenfunctions as $V_{h}=[H_{h},U_{h}]^{T}$ , where $H_{h}$ , $U_{h}$ are elements
of $S_{h}^{2}$ , $S_{h,0}^{2}$ , respectively, regarded as vector spaces over $\mathbb{C}$ , we have $L_{h}U_{h}=-\lambda_{h}H_{h}$ , $\widetilde{L}_{h}H_{h}=-\lambda_{h}M_{h}U_{h}$ , from which $\displaystyle(L_{h}U_{h},H_{h})$ $\displaystyle=-\lambda_{h}\|H_{h}\|^{2}\,,$ (4.27) $\displaystyle(\widetilde{L}_{h}H_{h},U_{h})$ $\displaystyle=-\lambda_{h}(M_{h}U_{h},U_{h})\,,$ where the $L^{2}$ inner product for complex valued functions is defined as $(f,g)=\int_{0}^{1}f(x)\overline{g(x)}dx$ . Now $(L_{h}U_{h},H_{h})=(U_{hx},H_{h})=-(U_{h},H_{hx})=-\overline{(\widetilde{L}_{h}H_{h},U_{h})}\,.$ Therefore, from $(\ref{eq427})$ $\lambda_{h}=\frac{-2i\text{Im}(U_{hx},H_{h})}{\|H_{h}\|^{2}+a(U_{h},U_{h})}\,.$ (4.28) We conclude that the spectrum of $(\ref{eq426})$ consists of purely imaginary eigenvalues
(and is symmetric about the origin as $-\lambda_{h}$ is also an eigenvalue corresponding to
the eigenvector $\overline{V}\!_{h}$ ). Moreover, it follows from $(\ref{eq428})$ that $|\lambda_{h}|\leq\frac{2\|U_{hx}\|\|H_{h}\|}{\|H_{h}\|^{2}+a(U_{h},U_{h})}\leq C\,,$ i.e. that the spectrum is bounded by a constant $C$ independent of $h$ , implying that the
linearized semidiscrete problem is not stiff. As we saw in Proposition $4.1$ , when the nonlinear $(\ref{cb})$ or $(\ref{scb})$ system is
discetized by the explicit Euler-Galerkin method, the mesh condition $k=O(h^{2})$ is sufficient
for stability. In a numerical experiment, we solved the nonlinear, nonhomogeneous $(\ref{cb})$ system using as exact solution $\eta(x,t)=\exp(2t)(\cos(\pi x)+x+2)$ , $u(x,t)=\exp(xt)(\sin(\pi x)+x^{3}-x^{2})$ for $x\in[0,1]$ , and discretizing by the explicit
Euler-standard Galerkin method with piecewise linear functions and fixed $N=1/h=400$ . When we
integrated up to $T=1$ using $k=h^{2}$ , we obtained an $L^{2}$ error for $\eta$ that was
approximately equal to $2.2090(-4)$ . The accuracy degenerated when we took $k=h^{\alpha}$ with decreasing $\alpha<2$ . For example, at the temporal instance closest to $T=1$ the
computations yielded the following magnitudes of the $L^{2}$ errors of $\eta$ : $k=h^{1.8}$ $3.8839(-4)$ $k=h^{1.6}$ $1.1257(-3)$ $k=h^{1.4}$ $3.6917(-3)$ $k=h^{1.2}$ overflow at about $t=0.8$ . 4.2. The improved Euler method We next study the temporal discretization of the
initial-value problems $(\ref{eq27})$ , $(\ref{eq220})$ and $(\ref{eq29})$ , $(\ref{eq220})$ by
the explicit, second-order accurate ‘improved Euler’ scheme, that may be written in the case
of the o.d.e. $y^{\prime}=f(t,y)$ in the two-step form $\displaystyle y^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle=y^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}f(t^{n},y^{n})\,,$ $\displaystyle y^{n+1}$ $\displaystyle=y^{n}+kf(t^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2},y^{n,1})\,.$ Using notation analogous to that established in the previous paragraph, in the case of $(\ref{cb})$ we seek for $0\leq n\leq M$ $H_{h}^{n}\in S_{h}^{2}$ , $U_{h}^{n}\in S_{h,0}^{2}$ ,
and for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ $H_{h}^{n,1}\in S_{h}^{2}$ , $U_{h}^{n,1}\in S_{h,0}^{2}$ , such that $\displaystyle H_{h}^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle-H_{h}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}PU_{hx}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}P(H_{h}^{n}U_{h}^{n})_{x}=0\,,$ (4.29) $\displaystyle U_{h}^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle-U_{h}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}AH_{hx}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}A(U_{h}^{n}U_{hx}^{n})=0\,,$ $\displaystyle H_{h}^{n+1}$ $\displaystyle-H_{h}^{n}+kPU_{hx}^{n,1}+kP(H_{h}^{n,1}U_{h}^{n,1})_{x}=0\,,$ $\displaystyle U_{h}^{n+1}$ $\displaystyle-U_{h}^{n}+kAH_{hx}^{n,1}+kA(U_{h}^{n,1}U_{hx}^{n,1})=0\,,$ for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ , with $H_{h}^{0}=I_{h}\eta^{0}$ , $U_{h}^{0}=I_{h,0}u^{0}$ . In the case
of $(\ref{scb})$ the analogous equations are $\displaystyle H_{h}^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle-H_{h}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}PU_{hx}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{4}P(H_{h}^{n}U_{h}^{n})_{x}=0\,,$ (4.30) $\displaystyle U_{h}^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle-U_{h}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}AH_{hx}^{n}+\tfrac{3k}{4}A(U_{h}^{n}U_{hx}^{n})+\tfrac{k}{4}A(H_{h}^{n}H_{hx}^{n})=0\,,$ $\displaystyle H_{h}^{n+1}$ $\displaystyle-H_{h}^{n}+kPU_{hx}^{n,1}+\tfrac{k}{2}P(H_{h}^{n,1}U_{h}^{n,1})_{x}=0\,,$ $\displaystyle U_{h}^{n+1}$ $\displaystyle-U_{h}^{n}+kAH_{hx}^{n,1}+\tfrac{3k}{2}A(U_{h}^{n,1}U_{hx}^{n,1})+\tfrac{k}{2}A(H_{h}^{n,1}H_{hx}^{n,1})=0\,,$ for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ , with $H_{h}^{0}=I_{h}\eta^{0}$ , $U_{h}^{0}=I_{h,0}u^{0}$ . In order to study
the consistency and convergence of the schemes, we let again $H^{n}=H(t^{n})=I_{h}\eta(t^{n})$ , $U^{n}=U(t^{n})=I_{h,0}u(t^{n})$ , where $(\eta,u)$ is the solution of $(\ref{cb})$ or $(\ref{scb})$ , and define, in the case of $(\ref{scb})$ , $(H^{n,1},U^{n,1})\in S_{h}^{2}\times S_{h,0}^{2}$ for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ by the equations $\displaystyle H^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle-H^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}PU_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{4}P(H^{n}U^{n})_{x}=0\,,$ (4.31) $\displaystyle U^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle-U^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}AH_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{3k}{4}A(U^{n}U_{x}^{n})+\tfrac{k}{4}A(H^{n}H_{x}^{n})=0\,.$ In the case of $(\ref{cb})$ $H^{n,1}$ , $U^{n,1}$ are defined analogously. Our consistency
result is: Lemma 4.3 . Suppose that the solution $(\eta,u)$ of $(\ref{scb})$ is sufficiently smooth
and let $\lambda=k/h$ . Define, for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ , $\delta_{1}^{n}$ , $\delta_{2}^{n}$ by the equations $\displaystyle\delta_{1}^{n}$ $\displaystyle=H^{n+1}-H^{n}+kPU_{x}^{n,1}+\tfrac{k}{2}P(H^{n,1}U^{n,1})_{x}\,,$ (4.32) $\displaystyle\delta_{2}^{n}$ $\displaystyle=U^{n+1}-U^{n}+kAH_{x}^{n,1}+\tfrac{3k}{2}A(U^{n,1}U_{x}^{n,1})+\tfrac{k}{2}A(H^{n,1}H_{x}^{n,1})\,.$ (4.33) Then, there exists a constant $C_{1}=C_{1}(\lambda)$ , which is a polynomial of $\lambda$ of degree
one, such that $\max_{0\leq n\leq M-1}(\|\delta_{1}^{n}\|+\|\delta_{2}^{n}\|_{1})\leq C_{1}k(k^{2}+h^{3/2})\,.$ The analogous result holds for $(\ref{cb})$ as well. Proof. Let $0\leq n\leq M-1$ . By $(\ref{eq431})$ , $(\ref{eq49})$ and $(\ref{eq410})$ we have $H^{n,1}=H^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}H_{t}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\psi^{n}\,,\quad U^{n,1}=U^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}U_{t}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\zeta^{n}\,.$ (4.34) From these expressions, after some algebra, we obtain $H^{n,1}U^{n,1}=H^{n}U^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}(HU)_{t}^{n}+w_{1}^{n}\,,$ where $w_{1}^{n}:=\tfrac{k^{2}}{4}H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}(U^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}U_{t}^{n})\psi^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}(H^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}H_{t}^{n})\zeta^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{4}\psi^{n}\zeta^{n}\,.$ (4.35) Hence, by $(\ref{eq432})$ , $(\ref{eq434})$ , $(\ref{eq49})$ , and the above we obtain $\delta_{1}^{n}=H^{n+1}-H^{n}-kH_{t}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{2}}{2}H_{tt}^{n}+k\psi^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{2}\psi_{t}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{2}}{2}P\zeta_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}Pw_{1x}^{n}\,.$ (4.36) Now, $(\ref{eq435})$ , in view of $(\ref{eq411})$ and the approximation and inverse properties
of $S_{h}^{2}$ and $S_{h,0}^{2}$ , gives $\displaystyle\|w_{1}^{n}\|_{1}$ $\displaystyle\leq C(k^{2}\|H_{t}^{n}\|_{1}\|U_{t}^{n}\|_{1}+k\|\psi^{n}\|_{1}(\|U^{n}\|_{1}+k\|U_{t}^{n}\|_{1})$ $\displaystyle\qquad+k\|\zeta^{n}\|_{1}(\|H^{n}\|_{1}+k\|H_{t}^{n}\|_{1})+k^{2}\|\psi^{n}\|_{1}\|\zeta^{n}\|_{1})$ $\displaystyle\leq c(k^{2}+kh^{-1}h^{3/2}(1+ck)+kh^{2}(1+ck)+k^{2}h^{-1}h^{7/2})$ $\displaystyle\leq c(k^{2}+\lambda h^{3/2}).$ Therefore, by Taylor’s theorem and $(\ref{eq411})$ we have $\|\delta_{1}^{n}\|\leq c(k^{3}+kh^{3/2}+k^{2}h^{3/2}+k^{2}h^{2}+k(k^{2}+\lambda h^{3/2})\leq C_{1}k(k^{2}+h^{3/2})\,,$ (4.37) where $C_{1}$ is a constant that is a polynomial of $\lambda$ of degree one. (Such constants
will be generically denoted by $C_{1}$ in the sequel of this proof.) In order to estimate $\|\delta_{2}^{n}\|_{1}$ note that by $(\ref{eq434})$ $U^{n,1}U_{x}^{n,1}=U^{n}U_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}(UU_{x})_{t}^{n}+w_{2}^{n}\,,$ (4.38) where $w_{2}^{n}:=\tfrac{k^{2}}{4}U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\bigl((U^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}U_{t}^{n})\zeta^{n}\bigr)_{x}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{4}\zeta^{n}\zeta_{x}^{n}\,.$ By $(\ref{eq411})$ and the approximation properties of $S_{h,0}^{2}$ we have $\|w_{2}^{n}\|\leq C(k^{2}+kh^{2})\,.$ (4.39) Similarly, $H^{n,1}H_{x}^{n,1}=H^{n}H_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}(HH_{x})_{t}^{n}+w_{3}^{n}\,,$ (4.40) where $w_{3}^{n}:=\tfrac{k^{2}}{4}H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\bigl((H^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}H_{t}^{n})\psi^{n}\bigr)_{x}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{4}\psi^{n}\psi_{x}^{n}\,.$ By $(\ref{eq411})$ and the approximation and inverse properties of $S_{h}^{2}$ we have $\|w_{3}^{n}\|\leq C(k^{2}+\lambda h^{3/2})\,.$ (4.41) By $(\ref{eq433})$ , $(\ref{eq435})$ , $(\ref{eq438})$ , and $(\ref{eq440})$ , we see now that $\delta_{2}^{n}=(U^{n+1}-U^{n}-kU_{t}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{2}}{2}U_{tt}^{n})+k\zeta^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{2}\zeta_{t}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{2}}{2}A\psi_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{3k}{2}Aw_{2}^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}Aw_{3}^{n}\,.$ Therefore, by Taylor’s theorem, $(\ref{eq411})$ , $(\ref{eq46})$ , $(\ref{eq439})$ , $(\ref{eq441})$ , $\|\delta_{2}^{n}\|_{1}\leq c(k^{3}+kh^{2}+k^{2}h^{2}+k^{2}h^{3/2}+k^{3}+k^{2}h^{2}+k^{3}+\lambda kh^{3/2})\leq C_{1}k(k^{2}+h^{3/2})\,,$ which, with $(\ref{eq437})$ , concludes the proof of the Lemma. The case of $(\ref{cb})$ is
entirely analogous.
∎ For the stability and convergence of the fully discrete scheme it does not suffice to suppose
that $k=O(h)$ . The following result shows that the stronger condition $k=O(h^{4/3})$ is sufficient. Proposition 4.2 . Suppose that the solutions $(\eta,u)$ of $(\ref{scb})$ and $(\ref{cb})$ are sufficiently smooth on $[0,T]$ . Then, if $\mu=k/h^{4/3}$ , there is a constant $C=C(\mu)$ , which is an increasing continuous function of $\mu$ , such that $\max_{0\leq n\leq M}\|H_{h}^{n}-\eta(t^{n})\|\leq C(k^{2}+h^{3/2})\,,\quad\max_{0\leq n\leq M}\|U_{h}^{n}-u(t^{n})\|_{1}\leq C(k^{2}+h)\,.$ Proof. We consider $(\ref{scb})$ , and put $\varepsilon^{n}=H^{n}-H_{h}^{n}$ , $e^{n}=U^{n}-U_{h}^{n}$ , $\theta^{n}=H^{n,1}-H_{h}^{n,1}$ , and $\xi^{n}=U^{n,1}-U_{h}^{n,1}$ .
We will show that $\max_{0\leq n\leq M}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\leq C(k^{2}+h^{3/2})\,,$ (4.42) from which the conclusion of the proposition follows. From $(\ref{eq430})$ , $(\ref{eq432})$ , $(\ref{eq433})$ we have, for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ $\varepsilon^{n+1}=\varepsilon^{n}-kP\xi_{x}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}P(H^{n,1}U^{n,1}-H_{h}^{n,1}U_{h}^{n,1})_{x}+\delta_{1}^{n}\,,$ (4.43) and $e^{n+1}=e^{n}-kA\theta_{x}^{n}-\tfrac{3k}{2}A(U^{n,1}U_{x}^{n,1}-U_{h}^{n,1}U_{hx}^{n,1})-\tfrac{k}{2}A(H^{n,1}H_{x}^{n,1}-H_{h}^{n,1}H_{hx}^{n,1})+\delta_{2}^{n}\,.$ (4.44) From Lemma $4.3$ we have an estimate of $\|\delta_{1}^{n}\|+\|\delta_{2}^{n}\|_{1}$ .
Our goal is to obtain suitable estimates of the remaining terms of the right-hand sides of $(\ref{eq443})$ and $(\ref{eq444})$ in terms of $\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1}$ . To do this,
note first that $(\ref{eq431})$ and $(\ref{eq430})$ give $\theta^{n}=\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}Pe_{x}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{4}P(H^{n}U^{n}-H_{h}^{n}U_{h}^{n})_{x}\,.$ But $H^{n}U^{n}-H_{h}^{n}U_{h}^{n}=H^{n}e^{n}-\varepsilon^{n}e^{n}+U^{n}\varepsilon^{n}$ . Therefore $\theta^{n}=\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{4}\rho_{1}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\omega_{1}^{n}\,,$ (4.45) where $\rho_{1}^{n}:=P(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}\,,$ (4.46) and $\omega_{1}^{n}:=Pe_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}P(H^{n}e^{n})_{x}-\tfrac{1}{2}P(\varepsilon^{n}e^{n})_{x}\,.$ (4.47) Similarly, $\xi^{n}=e^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}A\varepsilon_{x}^{n}-\tfrac{3k}{4}A(U^{n}U_{x}^{n}-U_{h}^{n}U_{hx}^{n})-\tfrac{k}{4}A(H^{n}H_{x}^{n}-H_{h}^{n}H_{hx}^{n})\,.$ But $U^{n}U_{x}^{n}-U_{h}U_{hx}^{n}=(U^{n}e^{n})_{x}-e^{n}e_{x}^{n}$ , $H^{n}H_{x}^{n}-H_{h}^{n}H_{hx}^{n}=(H^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}-\varepsilon^{n}\varepsilon_{x}^{n}$ .
Therefore $\xi^{n}=e^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\omega_{2}^{n}\,,$ (4.48) where $\omega_{2}^{n}:=A\varepsilon_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{3}{2}A(U^{n}e^{n})_{x}-\tfrac{3}{2}A(e^{n}e_{x}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(H^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}-\tfrac{1}{2}A(\varepsilon^{n}\varepsilon_{x}^{n})\,.$ (4.49) In addition, by $(\ref{eq445})$ we have $H^{n,1}U^{n,1}-H_{h}^{n,1}U_{h}^{n,1}=U^{n}\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{4}U^{n}\rho_{1}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}U^{n}\omega_{1}^{n}+\omega_{3}^{n}\,,$ (4.50) where $\omega_{3}^{n}:=(U^{n,1}-U^{n})\theta^{n}+H^{n,1}\xi^{n}-\theta^{n}\xi^{n}\,.$ (4.51) From $(\ref{eq443})$ , $(\ref{eq445})$ - $(\ref{eq450})$ we conclude therefore that for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ $\varepsilon^{n+1}=\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{8}\rho_{2}^{n}-k\omega_{4}^{n}+\delta_{1}^{n}\,,$ (4.52) where $\rho_{2}^{n}:=P(U^{n}\rho_{1}^{n})_{x}\,,$ (4.53) and $\omega_{4}^{n}:=Pe_{x}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}P\omega_{2x}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{4}P(U^{n}\omega_{1}^{n})_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}P\omega_{3x}^{n}\,.$ (4.54) Finally, using the identities $U^{n,1}U_{x}^{n,1}-U_{h}^{n,1}U_{hx}^{n,1}=(U^{n,1}\xi^{n})_{x}-\xi^{n}\xi_{x}^{n}$ and $H^{n,1}H_{x}^{n,1}-H_{h}^{n,1}H_{hx}^{n,1}=(H^{n,1}\theta^{n})_{x}-\theta^{n}\theta_{x}^{n}$ ,
we obtain from $(\ref{eq444})$ that for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ $e^{n+1}=e^{n}-kA\theta_{x}^{n}-\tfrac{3k}{2}A\bigl((U^{n,1}\xi^{n})_{x}-\xi^{n}\xi_{x}^{n}\bigr)-\tfrac{k}{2}A\bigl((H^{n,1}\theta^{n})_{x}-\theta^{n}\theta_{x}^{n}\bigr)+\delta_{2}^{n}\,.$ (4.55) We now estimate the various terms in the right-hand sides of $(\ref{eq452})$ and $(\ref{eq455})$ .
Let $0\leq n^{*}\leq M-1$ be the maximal index for which $\|\varepsilon^{n}\|_{1}+\|e^{n}\|_{1}\leq 1\,,\quad 0\leq n\leq n^{*}\,.$ (4.56) Then, by $(\ref{eq447})$ , the approximation properties of $S_{h}^{2}$ and $(\ref{eq456})$ ,
we have, for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ $\|\omega_{1}^{n}\|\leq\|e^{n}\|_{1}+C\|H^{n}\|_{1}\|e^{n}\|_{1}+C\|\varepsilon^{n}\|_{1}\|e^{n}\|_{1}\leq C\|e^{n}\|_{1}\,.$ (4.57) By $(\ref{eq449})$ , the approximation properties of $S_{h}^{2}$ , $S_{h,0}^{2}$ , and $(\ref{eq46})$ there follows for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ $\|\omega_{2}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1}\|e^{n}\|+\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|\varepsilon^{n}\|_{1}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|)\leq C(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|)\,.$ (4.58) Hence, by $(\ref{eq448})$ , for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ $\|\xi^{n}\|_{1}\leq C(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ (4.59) In addition, by $(\ref{eq445})$ , $(\ref{eq446})$ , $(\ref{eq457})$ and the inverse assumptions
we have for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ $\|\theta^{n}\|\leq\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+C\tfrac{k}{2}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+C\|e^{n}\|_{1}\leq(1+C\lambda)\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+C\|e^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,,$ (4.60) where we have put again $\lambda=k/h$ ; in the sequel, $C_{\lambda}$ will denote various constants that
depend polynomially on $\lambda$ . Note also that in view of $(\ref{eq456})$ we have for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ , from $(\ref{eq457})$ , $(\ref{eq446})$ and inverse inequalities $\|\theta^{n}\|_{1}\leq\|\varepsilon^{n}\|_{1}+Ck\|\rho_{1}^{n}\|_{1}+Ck\|\omega_{1}^{n}\|_{1}\leq\|\varepsilon^{n}\|_{1}+C\lambda\|\rho_{1}^{n}\|+C\lambda\|\omega_{1}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}\,.$ (4.61) By $(\ref{eq451})$ , $(\ref{eq434})$ , $(\ref{eq411})$ , $(\ref{eq460})$ , $(\ref{eq459})$ and $(\ref{eq456})$ we have for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ $\displaystyle\|\omega_{3}^{n}\|$ $\displaystyle\leq C(\|U^{n,1}-U^{n}\|_{1}\|\theta^{n}\|+\|H^{n,1}\|\|\xi^{n}\|_{1}+\|\theta^{n}\|\|\xi^{n}\|_{1})$ (4.62) $\displaystyle\leq C((k+h^{2})\|\theta^{n}\|+\|\xi^{n}\|_{1}+\|\theta^{n}\|\|\xi^{n}\|_{1})$ $\displaystyle\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ Also, by $(\ref{eq460})$ , $(\ref{eq459})$ , $(\ref{eq461})$ , for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ $\displaystyle\|\omega_{3}^{n}\|_{1}$ $\displaystyle\leq C(\|U^{n,1}-U^{n}\|_{1}\|\theta^{n}\|_{1}+\|H^{n,1}\|_{1}\|\xi^{n}\|_{1}+\|\theta^{n}\|_{1}\|\xi^{n}\|_{1})$ (4.63) $\displaystyle\leq(k+h^{2})h^{-1}C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})+C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})+C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})$ $\displaystyle\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ Hence, by $(\ref{eq454})$ , $(\ref{eq458})$ , $(\ref{eq457})$ , $(\ref{eq462})$ and the inverse
inequalities we have for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ $\|\omega_{4}^{n}\|\leq\|e^{n}\|_{1}+Ck(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|)+C\lambda\|e^{n}\|_{1}+C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ Therefore, in the right-hand side of $(\ref{eq452})$ we have for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ in view
of Lemma $4.3$ , $\|-k\omega_{4}^{n}+\delta_{1}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}k(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})+C_{\lambda}k(k+h^{3/2})\,.$ (4.64) We embark now upon obtaining a sharp $L^{2}$ -estimate of the remaining term $\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{8}\rho_{2}^{n}$ in $(\ref{eq452})$ . We have $\|\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{8}\rho_{2}^{n}\|^{2}=\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{4}\|\rho_{1}^{n}\|^{2}+\tfrac{k^{4}}{64}\|\rho_{2}^{n}\|^{2}-k(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})+\tfrac{k^{2}}{4}(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})-\tfrac{k^{3}}{8}(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})\,.$ Now, by $(\ref{eq446})$ $(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})=(\varepsilon^{n},(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x})=\tfrac{1}{2}(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\varepsilon^{n})\,.$ Also, by $(\ref{eq453})$ , $(\ref{eq446})$ $\displaystyle(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=-(\varepsilon_{x}^{n},U^{n}\rho_{1}^{n})=-\|\rho_{1}^{n}\|^{2}+(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})\,,$ $\displaystyle(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})\,.$ We conclude that $\|\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{8}\rho_{2}^{n}\|^{2}=\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}+\tfrac{k^{4}}{64}\|\rho_{2}^{n}\|^{2}-\tfrac{k}{2}(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\varepsilon^{n})+\tfrac{k^{2}}{4}(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})-\tfrac{k^{3}}{16}(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})\,.$ (4.65) Now, by the approximation and inverse properties of $S_{h,0}^{2}$ we have by $(\ref{eq446})$ , $(\ref{eq453})$ $\displaystyle|(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\varepsilon^{n})|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\,,$ $\displaystyle|(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\|\varepsilon^{n}\|\|\rho_{1}^{n}\|\leq Ch^{-1}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\,,$ $\displaystyle|(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\|\rho_{1}^{n}\|^{2}\leq Ch^{-2}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\,,$ $\displaystyle\|\rho_{2}^{n}\|$ $\displaystyle\leq Ch^{-2}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|\,.$ Inserting these estimates in $(\ref{eq465})$ and recalling that $\mu=k/h^{4/3}$ we are led to
the inequality $\|\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{8}\rho_{2}^{n}\|^{2}\leq(1+Ck\mu^{3}+Ck+Ck\lambda+Ck\lambda^{2})\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\leq(1+C_{\mu}k)\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\,,$ and, hence, $\|\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{8}\rho_{2}^{n}\|\leq(1+C_{\mu}k)\|\varepsilon^{n}\|\,,$ (4.66) where, by $C_{\mu}$ we denote a constant depending polynomially on $\mu$ ; we have replaced $C_{\lambda}$ ’s by $C_{\mu}$ ’s since $\lambda=h^{1/3}\mu\leq\mu$ .
We finally obtain from $(\ref{eq466})$ , $(\ref{eq464})$ and $(\ref{eq452})$ , for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ ,
that $\|\varepsilon^{n+1}\|\leq\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+C_{\mu}k(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})+C_{\mu}k(k^{2}+h^{3/2})\,.$ (4.67) We now estimate the $\|\cdot\|_{1}$ norm of the various terms in the right-hand side
of $(\ref{eq455})$ . For $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ , by $(\ref{eq46})$ , $(\ref{eq434})$ , $(\ref{eq456})$ ,
and $(\ref{eq459})$ we have $\|A\bigl((U^{n,1}\xi^{n})_{x}-\xi^{n}\xi_{x}^{n}\bigr)\|_{1}\leq C\|U^{n,1}\xi^{n}-\tfrac{1}{2}(\xi^{n})^{2}\|\leq C\|\xi^{n}\|_{1}\leq C(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ Similarly, for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ , by $(\ref{eq460})$ , we have $\|A\bigl((H^{n,1}\theta^{n})_{x}-\theta^{n}\theta_{x}^{n}\bigr)\|_{1}\leq C\|H^{n,1}\theta^{n}-\tfrac{1}{2}(\theta^{n})^{2}\|\leq C_{\lambda}\|\theta^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ Therefore, using $(\ref{eq455})$ , $(\ref{eq460})$ and Lemma $4.3$ we have for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ $\displaystyle\|e^{n+1}\|_{1}$ $\displaystyle\leq\|e^{n}\|_{1}+Ck\|\theta^{n}\|+C_{\lambda}k(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})+C_{\lambda}k(k^{2}+h^{3/2})$ (4.68) $\displaystyle\leq\|e^{n}\|_{1}+C_{\lambda}k(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})+C_{\lambda}k(k^{2}+h^{3/2})\,.$ Adding now $(\ref{eq467})$ and $(\ref{eq468})$ , we conclude for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ that $\|\varepsilon^{n+1}\|+\|e^{n+1}\|_{1}\leq(1+C_{\mu}k)(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})+C_{\mu}k(k^{2}+h^{3/2})\,.$ Using Gronwall’s Lemma and taking $h$ sufficiently small we conclude that $n^{*}$ may be taken equal to $M-1$ and there holds that $\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1}\leq\exp(C_{\mu}T)(k^{2}+h^{3/2})\,,\quad 0\leq n\leq M\,,$ i.e. that $(\ref{eq442})$ is valid; the conclusion of the proposition follows.
∎ Remark 4.3 . Arguing as in Remark $4.1$ we have again $\|U_{h}^{n}-u(t^{n})\|_{\infty}=O(k^{2}+h^{3/2})$ . Remark 4.4 . If we repeat the numerical experiment in Remark $4.2$ using the improved Euler method
for time stepping, we obtain, for $N=1/h=400$ , the evolution of the $L^{2}$ errors $\|H_{h}^{n}-\eta(t^{n})\|$ when $k=h$ and $k=h^{4/3}$ shown in Table 4.1 .
These results suggest that the condition $k=h^{4/3}$ is probably necessary as well for the
stability of the scheme. If we repeat the experiment using $k=h$ and the fourth-order explicit
classical R-K scheme for time stepping (this scheme, as will be shown in the next paragraph, is
stable when $k=h$ ,) we obtain errors approximately equal to those of the last column of Table 4.1 :
For example at $t^{n}=0.95$ and $t^{n}=1.0$ the analogous errors are equal to $0.1810(-3)$ and $0.1954(-3)$ , respectively. This implies that the errors of the last column of Table 4.1 are essentially due to the spatial discretization. $t^{n}$ $k=h$ $t^{n}$ $k=h^{4/3}$ $0.05$ $0.9594(-5)$ $0.05090$ $0.9833(-5)$ $0.1$ $0.1857(-4)$ $0.10179$ $0.1890(-4)$ $0.3$ $0.5436(-4)$ $0.30198$ $0.5394(-4)$ $0.5$ $0.8538(-4)$ $0.50217$ $0.8393(-4)$ $0.7$ $0.1211(-3)$ $0.70236$ $0.1206(-3)$ $0.8$ $0.3949(-3)$ $0.80075$ $0.1448(-3)$ $0.825$ $0.2214(-2)$ $0.82450$ $0.1507(-3)$ $0.85$ $0.1445(-1)$ $0.85165$ $0.1572(-3)$ $0.9$ $0.8082$ $0.90254$ $0.1691(-3)$ $0.95$ $0.7706(+18)$ $0.95005$ $0.1811(-3)$ $1.0$ $overflow$ $1.00026$ $0.1963(-3)$ Table 4.1. $L^{2}$ -errors $\|H_{h}^{n}-\eta(t^{n})\|$ for the standard Galerkin method
