with $k=h$ and $k=h^{4/3}$ ; example of Remark $4.2$ . 4.3. Fourth-order Runge-Kutta scheme with cubic splines Our third example is time stepping with the classical, fourth-order accurate four-stage
explicit Runge-Kutta scheme, written in the case of the o.d.e. $y^{\prime}=f(t,y)$ in the three-step form $\displaystyle y^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle=y^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}f(t^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2},y^{n})\,,$ $\displaystyle y^{n,2}$ $\displaystyle=y^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}f(t^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2},y^{n,1})\,,$ $\displaystyle y^{n,3}$ $\displaystyle=y^{n}+kf(t^{n}+k,y^{n,2})\,,$ $\displaystyle y^{n+1}$ $\displaystyle=y^{n}+k\bigl(\tfrac{1}{6}f(t^{n},y^{n})+\tfrac{1}{3}f(t^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2},y^{n,1})+\tfrac{1}{3}f(t^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2},y^{n,2})+\tfrac{1}{6}f(t^{n}+k,y^{n,3})\bigr)\,.$ We will couple this scheme with a space discretization that uses cubic splines on a uniform mesh
on $[0,1]$ . We recall that the semidiscrete problem can be written, e.g. in the case of the $(\ref{scb})$ ,
in the form $(\ref{eq38})$ or, equivalently, as follows. We seek $(\eta_{h},u_{h})$ $\in C^{1}(0,T;S_{h}^{4}\times S_{h,0}^{4})$ such that $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \eta_{ht}&+Pu_{hx}+\tfrac{1}{2}P(\eta_{h}u_{h})_{x}=0\,,\\
u_{ht}&+A\eta_{hx}+\tfrac{3}{2}A(u_{h}u_{hx})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(\eta_{h}\eta_{hx})=0\,,\end{aligned}\quad 0\leq t\leq T\,,$ (4.69) $\displaystyle\eta_{h}(0)=I_{h}\eta_{0}\,,\quad u_{h}(0)=R_{h}u_{0}\,,$ where $P:L^{2}\to S_{h}^{4}$ is the $L^{2}$ -projection operator onto $S_{h}^{4}$ , $A:L^{2}\to S_{h,0}^{4}$ is defined as in $(\ref{eq45})$ , but now in $S_{h,0}^{4}$ , $I_{h}$ is the interpolant in $S_{h}^{4}$ , and $R_{h}$ is introduced in Paragraph $3.1$ .
The semidiscrete scheme for the $(\ref{cb})$ system is defined analogously. We start with an estimation of the truncation errors of the semidiscrete equations when applied to $I_{h}\eta(t)$ and $R_{h}u(t)$ similar to that of Lemma $4.1$ . Lemma 4.4 . Suppose that the solution $(\eta_{h},u_{h})$ of $(\ref{scb})$ is sufficiently smooth
in $[0,T]$ . Let $H(t)=I_{h}\eta(t)$ , $U(t)=R_{h}u(t)$ and define $\psi=\psi(t)\in S_{h}^{4}$ , $\zeta=\zeta(t)\in S_{h,0}^{4}$ , for $0\leq t\leq T$ by $\displaystyle H_{t}+PU_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}P(HU)_{x}$ $\displaystyle=\psi\,,$ (4.70) $\displaystyle U_{t}+AH_{x}+\tfrac{3}{2}A(UU_{x})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(HH_{x})$ $\displaystyle=\zeta\,.$ (4.71) Then, for $j=0$ , $1$ , $2$ , $3$ $\|\partial_{t}^{j}\psi\|\leq Ch^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}\,,\qquad\|\partial_{t}^{j}\zeta\|_{1}\leq Ch^{4}\,,$ (4.72) hold for $0\leq t\leq T$ . An analogous result holds for $(\ref{cb})$ as well. Proof. Subtracting the equations $\displaystyle P\eta_{t}+Pu_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}P(\eta u)_{x}$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ $\displaystyle H_{t}+PU_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}P(HU)_{x}$ $\displaystyle=\psi\,,$ we obtain, putting $\rho:=\eta-H$ , $\sigma:=u-U$ $P\rho_{t}+P\sigma_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}P(\eta u-HU)_{x}=-\psi\,.$ Since $\eta u-HU=\eta(u-U)+U(\eta-H)=\eta(u-U)-(u-U)(\eta-H)+u(\eta-H)=\eta\sigma+u\rho-\rho\sigma\,,$ we have $P\rho_{t}+P\sigma_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}P(\eta\sigma)_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}P(u\rho)_{x}-\tfrac{1}{2}P(\rho\sigma)_{x}=-\psi\,,$ and conclude from Lemmas $3.2$ and $3.4$ that $\|\partial_{t}^{j}\psi\|\leq Ch^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}\,,\quad j=0,1,2,3\,,$ which is the first estimate in $(\ref{eq472})$ By the definition of $A$ we have $A(u_{t}-\tfrac{1}{3}u_{txx})=U_{t}\,.$ Subtracting now the equations $\displaystyle A(u_{t}-\tfrac{1}{3}u_{txx})+A\eta_{x}+\tfrac{3}{2}A(uu_{x})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(\eta\eta_{x})$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ $\displaystyle U_{t}+AH_{x}+\tfrac{3}{2}A(UU_{x})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(HH_{x})$ $\displaystyle=\zeta\,,$ we obtain $A\rho_{x}+\tfrac{3}{2}A(uu_{x}-UU_{x})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(\eta\eta_{x}-HH_{x})=-\zeta\,.$ But $uu_{x}-UU_{x}=(u\sigma)_{x}-\sigma\sigma_{x}\,,\quad\eta\eta_{x}-HH_{x}=(\eta\rho)_{x}-\rho\rho_{x}\,.$ Hence $A\rho_{x}+\tfrac{3}{2}\bigl(A(u\sigma)_{x}-A(\sigma\sigma_{x})\bigr)+\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl(A(\eta\rho)_{x}-A(\rho\rho_{x})\bigr)=-\zeta\,,$ and from $(\ref{eq46})$ and the approximation properties of $S_{h}^{4}$ , $S_{h,0}^{4}$ we conclude $\|\partial_{t}^{j}\zeta\|_{1}\leq Ch^{4}\,,$ for $j=0,1,2,3$ , thus proving the Lemma. The $(\ref{cb})$ case is entirely similar.
∎ We now define the fully discrete scheme. We consider only the case of $(\ref{scb})$ , that of $(\ref{cb})$ being analogous. We let as usual $M$ be a positive integer, $k=T/M$ and $t^{n}=nk$ , for $n=0,1,\dots,M$ .
For $0\leq n\leq M$ we seek $(H_{h}^{n},U_{h}^{n})\in S_{h}^{4}\times S_{h,0}^{4}$ approximations of $\eta(t^{n})$ , $u(t^{n})$ , and for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ $(H_{h}^{n,j},U_{h}^{n,j})\in S_{h}^{4}\times S_{h,0}^{4}$ , $j=1,2,3$ , such that for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ $\displaystyle H_{h}^{n,j}-H_{h}^{n}+ka_{j}P\bigl(U_{h}^{n,j-1}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{h}^{n,j-1}U_{h}^{n,j-1}\bigr)_{x}$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ (4.73) $\displaystyle U_{h}^{n,j}-U_{h}^{n}+ka_{j}A\bigl(H_{hx}^{n,j-1}+\tfrac{3}{2}U_{h}^{n,j-1}U_{hx}^{n,j-1}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{h}^{n,j-1}H_{hx}^{n,j-1}\bigr)$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ for $j=1,2,3$ and $\displaystyle H_{h}^{n+1}-H_{h}^{n}+kP\Bigl[\sum_{j=1}^{4}b_{j}\bigl(U_{h}^{n,j-1}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{h}^{n,j-1}U_{h}^{n,j-1}\bigr)\Bigr]_{x}$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ (4.74) $\displaystyle U_{h}^{n+1}-U_{h}^{n}+kA\Bigl[\sum_{j=1}^{4}b_{j}\bigl(H_{hx}^{n,j-1}+\tfrac{3}{2}U_{hx}^{n,j-1}U_{h}^{n,j-1}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{hx}^{n,j-1}H_{h}^{n,j-1}\bigr)\Bigr]$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ where $H_{h}^{n,0}=H_{h}^{n}\,,\quad U_{h}^{n,0}=U_{h}^{n}\,,\quad a_{1}=a_{2}=1/2\,,\quad a_{3}=1\,,\quad b_{1}=b_{4}=1/6\,,\quad b_{2}=b_{3}=1/3\,,$ and $H_{h}^{0}=\eta_{h}(0)\,,\quad U_{h}^{0}=u_{h}(0)\,.$ We first investigate the consistency of this scheme. We define $H$ , $U$ as in Lemma $4.4$ and put $H^{n}=H(t^{n})$ , $U^{n}=U(t^{n})$ . We let $V^{n,j}\in S_{h}^{4}$ , $W^{n,j}\in S_{h,0}^{4}$ , for $j=0$ , $1$ , $2$ , $3$ be defined for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ by the equations $\displaystyle V^{n,j}-H^{n}+ka_{j}P\bigl(W^{n,j-1}+\tfrac{1}{2}V^{n,j-1}W^{n,j-1}\bigr)_{x}$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ (4.75) $\displaystyle W^{n,j}-U^{n}+ka_{j}A\bigl(V_{x}^{n,j-1}+\tfrac{3}{2}W^{n,j-1}W_{x}^{n,j-1}+\tfrac{1}{2}V^{n,j-1}V_{x}^{n,j-1}\bigr)$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ and $V^{n,0}=H^{n}\,,\quad W^{n,0}=U^{n}\,.$ Before proving our consistency estimates we introduce some notation that will simplify the computations.
Let $\Phi=U+\tfrac{1}{2}HU\,,\quad F=H_{x}+\tfrac{3}{2}UU_{x}+\tfrac{1}{2}HH_{x}\,,\quad\Phi^{n}=\Phi(t^{n})\,,\quad F^{n}=F(t^{n})\,,$ and consider the functions $\displaystyle\Phi_{h}^{n,j}$ $\displaystyle=U_{h}^{n,j}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{h}^{n,j}U_{h}^{n,j}\,,$ $\displaystyle F_{h}^{n,j}$ $\displaystyle=H_{hx}^{n,j}+\tfrac{3}{2}U_{h}^{n,j}U_{hx}^{n,j}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{h}^{n,j}H_{hx}^{n,j}\,,$ for $j=0$ , $1$ , $2$ , $3$ . With a slight abuse of notation we put for $j=0$ , $1$ , $2$ , $3$ $\displaystyle\Phi^{n,j}$ $\displaystyle=W^{n,j}+\tfrac{1}{2}V^{n,j}W^{n,j}\,,$ $\displaystyle F^{n,j}$ $\displaystyle=V_{x}^{n,j}+\tfrac{3}{2}W^{n,j}W_{x}^{n,j}+\tfrac{1}{2}V^{n,j}V_{x}^{n,j}\,.$ We may then write $(\ref{eq470})$ and $(\ref{eq471})$ as $H_{t}+P\Phi_{x}=\psi\,,$ (4.76) $U_{t}+AF=\zeta\,,$ (4.77) and $(\ref{eq473})$ , $(\ref{eq474})$ , respectively, as $\displaystyle H_{h}^{n,j}-H_{h}^{n}+ka_{j}P\Phi_{hx}^{n,j-1}$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ (4.78) $\displaystyle U_{h}^{n,j}-U_{h}^{n}+ka_{j}AF_{h}^{n,j-1}$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ for $j=1$ , $2$ , $3$ , and $\displaystyle H_{h}^{n+1}-H_{h}^{n}+kP\bigl[\sum_{j=1}^{4}b_{j}\Phi_{h}^{n,j-1}\bigr]_{x}=0\,,$ (4.79) $\displaystyle U_{h}^{n+1}-U_{h}^{n}+kA\bigl[\sum_{j=1}^{4}b_{j}F_{h}^{n,j-1}\bigr]=0\,.$ Finally, we may write the ralations $(\ref{eq475})$ in the form $\displaystyle V^{n,j}-H^{n}+ka_{j}P\Phi_{x}^{n,j-1}$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ (4.80) $\displaystyle W^{n,j}-U^{n}+ka_{j}AF^{n,j-1}$ $\displaystyle=0\,,$ for $j=1$ , $2$ , $3$ . Lemma 4.5 . Suppose that the solution $(\eta,u)$ of $(\ref{scb})$ is sufficiently smooth and let $\lambda=k/h$ . Define, for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ , $\delta_{1}^{n}$ and $\delta_{2}^{n}$ by the equations $\delta_{1}^{n}=H^{n+1}-H^{n}+kP\bigl[\sum_{j=1}^{4}b_{j}\Phi^{n,j-1}\bigr]_{x}\,,$ (4.81) $\delta_{2}^{n}=U^{n+1}-U^{n}+kA\bigl[\sum_{j=1}^{4}b_{j}F^{n,j-1}\bigr]\,.$ (4.82) Then, there exists a constant $C_{\lambda}$ which is a polynomial of $\lambda$ with nonnegative coefficients,
such that $\max_{0\leq n\leq M-1}\|\delta_{1}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}k(k^{4}+h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h})\,,\quad\max_{0\leq n\leq M-1}\|\delta_{2}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}k(k^{4}+h^{4})\,.$ Proof. We need to find formulas for the functions $V^{n,j}$ , $W^{n,j}$ , for $j=1$ , $2$ , $3$ up to some remainder terms.
From the first equation of $(\ref{eq477})$ we have $V^{n,1}-H^{n}+a_{1}kP\Phi_{x}^{n}=0\,,$ whence from $(\ref{eq473})$ $V^{n,1}=H^{n}+a_{1}kH_{t}^{n}-a_{1}k\psi^{n}\,.$ (4.83) Also, from the second equation of $(\ref{eq480})$ we obtain $W^{n,1}-U^{n}+a_{1}kAF^{n}=0\,,$ and so from $(\ref{eq477})$ $W^{n,1}=U^{n}+a_{1}kU_{t}^{n}-a_{1}k\zeta^{n}\,.$ (4.84) Thus $V^{n,1}W^{n,1}=H^{n}U^{n}+a_{1}k(HU)_{t}^{n}+a_{1}^{2}k^{2}H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n}+v_{1}^{n}\,,$ (4.85) where, in view of $(\ref{eq472})$ and inverse properties $\|v_{1}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}\,.$ In addition, from $(\ref{eq484})$ , $(\ref{eq485})$ we obtain $W^{n,1}+\tfrac{1}{2}V^{n,1}W^{n,1}=U^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}H^{n}U^{n}+a_{1}k\bigl(U_{t}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}(HU)_{t}^{n}\bigr)+\tfrac{a_{1}^{2}}{2}k^{2}H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n}+v_{2}^{n}\,,$ i.e. $\Phi^{n,1}=\Phi^{n}+a_{1}k\Phi_{t}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{1}^{2}}{2}k^{2}H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n}+v_{2}^{n}\,,$ (4.86) where $\|v_{2}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}\,.$ Since now $V^{n,2}=H^{n}-a_{2}kP\Phi_{x}^{n,1}\,,$ we have from $(\ref{eq486})$ that $V^{n,2}=H^{n}-a_{2}kP\Phi_{x}^{n}-a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}P\Phi_{xt}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}^{2}a_{2}}{2}k^{3}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}-a_{2}kP(v_{2}^{n})_{x}\,,$ from which, in view of $(\ref{eq473})$ , $(\ref{eq472})$ , and inverse properties there follows that $V^{n,2}=H^{n}+a_{2}kH_{t}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}H_{tt}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}^{2}a_{2}}{2}k^{3}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}+\psi_{1}^{n}\,,$ (4.87) with $\|\psi_{1}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}h^{4}\,,$ and $\|\psi_{1}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}\,.$ In addition, from $(\ref{eq484})$ $\displaystyle W^{n,1}W_{x}^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle=(U^{n}+a_{1}kU_{t}^{n}-a_{1}k\zeta^{n})(U_{x}^{n}+a_{1}kU_{tx}^{n}-a_{1}k\zeta_{x}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=U^{n}U_{x}^{n}+a_{1}k(UU_{x})_{t}^{n}+a_{1}^{2}k^{2}U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n}+w_{1}^{n}\,,$ where, in view of $(\ref{eq472})$ $\|w_{1}^{n}\|\leq Ckh^{4}\,.$ Similarly, from $(\ref{eq480})$ there follows $\displaystyle V^{n,1}V_{x}^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle=(H^{n}+a_{1}kH_{t}^{n}-a_{1}k\psi^{n})(H_{x}^{n}+a_{1}kH_{tx}^{n}-a_{1}k\psi_{x}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=H^{n}H_{x}^{n}+a_{1}k(HH_{x})_{t}^{n}+a_{1}^{2}k^{2}H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n}+w_{2}^{n}\,,$ where, in view of $(\ref{eq472})$ and inverse properties $\|w_{2}^{n}\|_{-1}\leq C_{\lambda}h^{4}\,,$ and $\|w_{2}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}\,.$ Therefore, from the above relations and $(\ref{eq483})$ we have $V_{x}^{n,1}+\tfrac{3}{2}W^{n,1}W_{x}^{n,1}+\tfrac{1}{2}V^{n,1}V_{x}^{n,1}=F^{n}+a_{1}kF_{t}^{n}+a_{1}^{2}k^{2}(\tfrac{3}{2}U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})+w_{3}^{n}\,,$ i.e. $F^{n,1}=F^{n}+a_{1}kF_{t}^{n}+a_{1}^{2}k^{2}(\tfrac{3}{2}U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})+w_{3}^{n}\,,$ (4.88) where $w_{3}^{n}=-k\psi_{1x}^{n}+\tfrac{3}{2}w_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}w_{2}^{n}\,.$ Since $W^{n,2}=U^{n}-a_{2}kAF^{n,1}\,,$ there follows from $(\ref{eq488})$ , $(\ref{eq477})$ , and previous estimates that $W^{n,2}=U^{n}+a_{2}kU_{t}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}U_{tt}^{n}-a_{1}^{2}a_{2}k^{3}\bigl[\tfrac{3}{2}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})\bigr]+\zeta_{1}^{n}\,,$ (4.89) where $\|\zeta_{1}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}kh^{4}\,.$ We now have from $(\ref{eq487})$ , $(\ref{eq489})$ , the fact that $a_{1}=a_{2}$ , and similar estimates
to those used above, that $\displaystyle V^{n,2}W^{n,2}=$ $\displaystyle\,\,H^{n}U^{n}+a_{2}k(HU)_{t}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}(H^{n}U_{tt}^{n}+H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n}+H_{tt}^{n}U^{n})+a_{1}a_{2}^{2}k^{3}(H_{t}^{n}U_{tt}^{n}+H_{tt}^{n}U_{t}^{n})$ $\displaystyle-a_{1}^{2}a_{2}k^{3}\bigl[\tfrac{3}{2}H^{n}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}H^{n}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})-\tfrac{1}{2}U^{n}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}\bigr]+v_{3}^{n}\,,$ where $\|v_{3}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}$ follows from similar, as above, estimates and from the stability of $P$ in $H^{1}$ . Rewriting the previous
equation as $\displaystyle V^{n,2}W^{n,2}=$ $\displaystyle\,\,H^{n}U^{n}+a_{2}k(HU)_{t}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}(HU)_{tt}^{n}-a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}^{2}k^{3}(H_{t}U_{t})_{t}^{n}$ $\displaystyle-a_{1}^{2}a_{2}k^{3}\bigl[\tfrac{3}{2}H^{n}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}H^{n}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}U^{n}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}\bigr]+v_{3}^{n}\,,$ we have, by the definition of $\Phi^{n,2}$ and $(\ref{eq489})$ $\displaystyle\Phi^{n,2}=$ $\displaystyle\Phi^{n}+a_{2}k\Phi_{t}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}\Phi_{tt}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}}{2}k^{2}H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}^{2}}{2}k^{3}(H_{t}U_{t})_{t}^{n}$ (4.90) $\displaystyle-a_{1}^{2}a_{2}k^{3}\bigl[\tfrac{3}{2}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{3}{4}H^{n}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{4}H^{n}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})$ $\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{4}U^{n}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}\bigr]+\zeta_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}v_{3}^{n}\,.$ Since now $V^{n,3}=H^{n}-a_{3}kP\Phi^{n,2}_{x}=H^{n}-kP\Phi^{n,2}_{x}\,,$ we have from $(\ref{eq490})$ and $(\ref{eq476})$ that $V^{n,3}=H^{n}+kH_{t}^{n}+a_{2}k^{2}H_{tt}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}H_{ttt}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}}{2}k^{3}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}+\psi_{2}^{n}\,,$ (4.91) where $\|\psi_{2}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}h^{4}\,,$ and (using the fact that $\|Af\|_{2}\leq C\|f\|$ for $f\in L^{2}$ ), $\|\psi_{2}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}\,.$ In addition, from $(\ref{eq489})$ $\displaystyle W^{n,2}W_{x}^{n,2}=$ $\displaystyle\,\Bigl[U^{n}+a_{2}kU_{t}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}U_{tt}^{n}-a_{1}^{2}a_{2}k^{3}\bigl[\tfrac{3}{2}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})\bigr]+\zeta_{1}^{n}\Bigr]\cdot$ $\displaystyle\,\Bigl[U_{x}^{n}+a_{2}kU_{tx}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}U_{ttx}^{n}-a_{1}^{2}a_{2}k^{3}\bigl[\tfrac{3}{2}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})\bigr]_{x}+\zeta_{1x}^{n}\Bigr]\,,$ and so $\displaystyle W^{n,2}W_{x}^{n,2}=$ $\displaystyle U^{n}U_{x}^{n}+a_{2}k(UU_{x})_{t}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}(UU_{x})_{tt}^{n}-a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}^{2}k^{3}(U_{t}^{n}U_{tt}^{n})_{x}$ (4.92) $\displaystyle-a_{1}^{2}a_{2}k^{3}\bigl[\tfrac{3}{2}U^{n}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}U^{n}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})\bigr]_{x}+w_{4}^{n}\,,$ where $\|w_{4}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}h^{4}$ . In addition, from $(\ref{eq487})$ $\displaystyle V^{n,2}V_{x}^{n,2}=$ $\displaystyle\,\bigl[H^{n}+a_{2}kH_{t}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}H_{tt}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}^{2}a_{2}}{2}k^{3}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}+\psi_{1}^{n}\bigr]\cdot$ $\displaystyle\,\bigl[H_{x}^{n}+a_{2}kH_{tx}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}H_{ttx}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}^{2}a_{2}}{2}k^{3}\bigl(P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}\bigr)_{x}+\psi_{1x}^{n}\bigr]\,.$ Hence, $\displaystyle V^{n,2}V_{x}^{n,2}=$ $\displaystyle H^{n}H_{x}^{n}+a_{2}k(HH_{x})_{t}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}(HH_{x})_{tt}^{n}-a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n}$ (4.93) $\displaystyle+a_{1}a_{2}^{2}k^{3}(H_{t}^{n}H_{tt}^{n})_{x}-\tfrac{a_{1}^{2}a_{2}}{2}k^{3}\bigl[H^{n}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}\bigr]_{x}+v_{4}^{n}\,,$ where $\|v_{4}^{n}\|_{-1}\leq C_{\lambda}h^{4}\,,$ and $\|v_{4}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}\,.$ From the definition of $F^{n,2}$ , $(\ref{eq487})$ , $(\ref{eq492})$ and $(\ref{eq493})$ we have $\displaystyle F^{n,2}=$ $\displaystyle F^{n}+a_{2}kF_{t}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}F_{tt}^{n}-a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}(\tfrac{3}{2}U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})$ (4.94) $\displaystyle+a_{1}a_{2}^{2}k^{3}\bigl[\tfrac{3}{2}U_{t}^{n}U_{tt}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{t}^{n}H_{tt}^{n}-\tfrac{1}{2}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}-\tfrac{9}{4}U^{n}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})$ $\displaystyle-\tfrac{3}{4}U^{n}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})-\tfrac{1}{4}H^{n}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}\bigr]_{x}+w_{5}^{n}\,,$ where $w_{5}^{n}=\psi_{1x}^{n}+\tfrac{3}{2}w_{4}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}v_{4}^{n}\,.$ Hence, since $W^{n,3}=U^{n}-kAF^{n,2}\,,$ we obtain $\displaystyle W^{n,3}=$ $\displaystyle U^{n}+kU_{t}^{n}+a_{2}k^{2}U_{tt}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}U_{ttt}^{n}$ (4.95) $\displaystyle+a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}\bigl[\tfrac{3}{2}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})\bigr]+\zeta_{2}^{n}\,,$ where $\|\zeta_{2}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}kh^{4}+Ck^{4}\,.$ From $(\ref{eq491})$ , $(\ref{eq495})$ we get $\displaystyle V^{n,3}W^{n,3}=$ $\displaystyle\,H^{n}U^{n}+k(HU)_{t}^{n}+a_{2}k^{2}(HU)_{tt}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}(HU)_{ttt}^{n}-a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}(H_{t}U_{t})_{t}^{n}$ $\displaystyle\,+a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}\bigl[\tfrac{3}{2}H^{n}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}H^{n}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}U^{n}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}\bigr]+v_{5}^{n}\,,$ where $\|v_{5}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}\,.$ Hence, from the definition of $\Phi^{n,3}$ , $(\ref{eq495})$ and the last relation, we see that $\displaystyle\Phi^{n,3}=$ $\displaystyle\Phi^{n}+k\Phi_{t}^{n}+a_{2}k^{2}\Phi_{tt}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}\Phi_{ttt}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}}{2}k^{3}(H_{t}U_{t})_{t}^{n}$ (4.96) $\displaystyle+a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}\bigl[\tfrac{3}{2}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{3}{4}H^{n}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})$ $\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{4}H^{n}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{4}U^{n}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}\bigr]+\zeta_{2}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}v_{5}^{n}\,.$ We are now ready to estimate $\delta_{1}^{n}$ . By $(\ref{eq490})$ and $(\ref{eq496})$ we have $\displaystyle b_{3}\Phi^{n,2}+b_{4}\Phi^{n,3}=$ $\displaystyle\,\,(b_{3}+b_{4})\Phi^{n}+(b_{3}a_{2}+b_{4})k\Phi_{t}^{n}+(a_{1}a_{2}b_{3}+a_{2}b_{4})k^{2}\Phi_{tt}^{n}$ $\displaystyle+a_{1}a_{2}b_{4}k^{3}\Phi_{ttt}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}b_{3}}{2}k^{2}H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n}+b_{3}(\zeta_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}v_{3}^{n})+b_{4}(\zeta_{2}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}v_{5}^{n})\,.$ In addition, in view of $(\ref{eq486})$ $b_{1}\Phi^{n}+b_{2}\Phi^{n,1}=(b_{1}+b_{2})\Phi^{n}+a_{1}b_{2}k\Phi_{t}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{1}^{2}b_{2}}{2}k^{2}H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n}+b_{2}v_{2}^{n}\,.$ Hence, $\sum_{j=1}^{4}b_{j}\Phi^{n,j-1}=\Phi^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}\Phi_{t}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{6}\Phi_{tt}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{3}}{24}\Phi_{ttt}^{n}+v_{6}^{n}\,,$ where $v_{6}^{n}=b_{2}v_{2}^{n}+b_{3}(\zeta_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}v_{3}^{n})+b_{4}(\zeta_{2}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}v_{5}^{n})\,.$ Therefore, by $(\ref{eq481})$ and $(\ref{eq476})$ we obtain $\delta_{1}^{n}=H^{n+1}-H^{n}-kH_{t}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{2}}{2}H_{tt}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{3}}{6}H_{ttt}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{4}}{24}H_{tttt}^{n}+kP(v_{6}^{n})_{x}+v_{7}^{n}\,,$ where, by $(\ref{eq472})$ $\|v_{7}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}kh^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}\,.$ Since, in addition $\|v_{6}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}\,,$ we conclude that $\|\delta_{1}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}k(k^{4}+h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h})\,.$ To estimate $\delta_{2}^{n}$ we must also compute $F^{n,3}$ . To this end, using $(\ref{eq495})$ we have
after some algebra $\displaystyle W^{n,3}W_{x}^{n,3}=$ $\displaystyle U^{n}U_{x}^{n}+k(UU_{x})_{t}^{n}+a_{2}k^{2}(UU_{x})_{tt}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}(UU_{x})_{ttt}^{n}-a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}(U_{t}^{n}U_{tt}^{n})_{x}$ (4.97) $\displaystyle+a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}[\tfrac{3}{2}U^{n}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}U^{n}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})]_{x}+w_{6}^{n}\,,$ where $\|w_{6}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}h^{4}\,.$ Similarly, from $(\ref{eq491})$ , we have $\displaystyle V^{n,3}V_{x}^{n,3}=$ $\displaystyle\,H^{n}H_{x}^{n}+k(HH_{x})_{t}^{n}+a_{2}k^{2}(HH_{x})_{tt}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}(HH_{x})_{ttt}^{n}$ $\displaystyle-a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}(H_{t}^{n}H_{tt}^{n})_{x}+\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}}{2}k^{3}[H^{n}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}]_{x}+v_{7}^{n}\,,$ where $\|v_{7}^{n}\|_{-1}\leq C_{\lambda}h^{4}\,,\qquad\|v_{7}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h}\,.$ Hence, from the definition of $F^{n,3}$ , $(\ref{eq491})$ , $(\ref{eq497})$ and the last relation,
we obtain $\displaystyle F^{n,3}=$ $\displaystyle F^{n}+kF_{t}^{n}+a_{2}k^{2}F_{tt}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}F_{ttt}^{n}-a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}[\tfrac{3}{2}U_{t}^{n}U_{tt}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{t}^{n}H_{tt}^{n}]_{x}$ (4.98) $\displaystyle+a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}[\tfrac{1}{2}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}+\tfrac{9}{4}U^{n}A(U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n})+\tfrac{3}{4}U^{n}A(H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})$ $\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{4}H^{n}P(H_{t}^{n}U_{t}^{n})_{x}\bigr]_{x}+w_{7}^{n}\,,$ where $w_{7}^{n}=\psi_{2x}^{n}+\tfrac{3}{2}w_{6}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}v_{7}^{n}\,.$ We may now estimate $\delta_{2}^{n}$ . By $(\ref{eq494})$ and $(\ref{eq498})$ we obtain $\displaystyle b_{3}F^{n,2}+b_{4}F^{n,3}$ $\displaystyle=(b_{3}+b_{4})F^{n}+(a_{2}b_{3}+b_{4})kF_{t}^{n}+(a_{1}a_{2}b_{3}+a_{2}b_{4})k^{2}F_{tt}^{n}+a_{1}a_{2}b_{4}k^{3}F_{ttt}^{n}$ $\displaystyle\quad-a_{1}a_{2}b_{3}k^{2}(\tfrac{3}{2}U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{t}^{n}H_{tx}^{n})+b_{3}w_{5}^{n}+b_{4}w_{7}^{n}\,.$ Also, in view of $(\ref{eq488})$ $b_{1}F^{n}+b_{2}F^{n,1}=(b_{1}+b_{2})F^{n}+a_{1}b_{2}kF_{t}^{n}+a_{1}^{2}b_{2}k^{2}(\tfrac{3}{2}U_{t}^{n}U_{tx}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}H_{t}H_{tx}^{n})+b_{2}w_{3}^{n}\,.$ Therefore $\sum_{j=1}^{4}b_{j}F^{n,j-1}=F^{n}+\tfrac{k}{2}F_{t}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{6}F_{tt}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{3}}{24}F_{ttt}^{n}+w_{8}^{n}\,,$ where $w_{8}^{n}=b_{2}w_{3}^{n}+b_{3}w_{5}^{n}+b_{4}w_{7}^{n}\,.$ Hence, from $(\ref{eq482})$ and $(\ref{eq477})$ we obtain $\delta_{2}^{n}=U^{n+1}-U^{n}-kU_{t}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{2}}{2}U_{tt}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{3}}{6}U_{ttt}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{4}}{24}U_{tttt}^{n}+kAw_{8}^{n}+w_{9}^{n}\,,$ where, by $(\ref{eq472})$ $\|w_{9}^{n}\|_{1}\leq Ckh^{4}\,.$ Since we also have, by previous estimates, $\|w_{8}^{n}\|_{-1}\leq C_{\lambda}h^{4}\,,$ we finally conclude that $\|\delta_{2}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}k(k^{4}+h^{4})\,,$ and the proof of the Lemma $4.5$ is completed.
∎ We show stability and convergence of the fully discrete scheme in the course of the proof of
the following result. Proposition 4.3 . Suppose that the solution $(\eta,u)$ of $(\ref{scb})$ is sufficiently smooth
on $[0,T]$ . Let $\lambda=k/h$ and $(H_{h}^{n},U_{h}^{n})$ be the solution of $(\ref{eq473})$ - $(\ref{eq474})$ .
Then, there exists a positive constant $\lambda_{0}$ and a constant $C$ independent of $k$ and h, such that
for $\lambda\leq\lambda_{0}$ , $\max_{0\leq n\leq M}\|\eta(t^{n})-H_{h}^{n}\|\leq C(k^{4}+h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h})\,,\qquad\quad\max_{0\leq n\leq M}\|u(t^{n})-U_{h}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C(k^{4}+h^{3})\,.$ Proof. It suffices to show that $\max_{0\leq n\leq M}(\|H^{n}-H_{h}^{n}\|+\|U^{n}-U_{h}^{n}\|_{1})\leq C(k^{4}+h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h})\,.$ Let $\varepsilon^{n}=H^{n}-H_{h}^{n}\,,\quad e^{n}=U^{n}-U_{h}^{n}\,,\quad\varepsilon^{n,j}=V^{n,j}-H_{h}^{n,j}\,,\quad e^{n,j}=W^{n,j}-U_{h}^{n,j}\,,\quad j=1,2,3\,.$ Then, by $(\ref{eq475})$ and $(\ref{eq478})$ , we have for $j=1,2,3$ $\varepsilon^{n,j}=\varepsilon^{n}-ka_{j}P\bigl(\Phi^{n,j-1}-\Phi_{h}^{n,j-1}\bigr)_{x}\,,$ (4.99) $e^{n,j}=e^{n}-ka_{j}A\bigl(F^{n,j-1}-F_{h}^{n,j-1}\bigr)\,,$ (4.100) and by $(\ref{eq479})$ , $(\ref{eq481})$ , and $(\ref{eq482})$ $\varepsilon^{n+1}=\varepsilon^{n}-k\sum_{j=1}^{4}b_{j}P\bigl(\Phi^{n,j-1}-\Phi_{h}^{n,j-1}\bigr)_{x}+\delta_{1}^{n}\,,$ (4.101) $e^{n+1}=e^{n}-k\sum_{j=1}^{4}b_{j}A\bigl(F^{n,j-1}-F_{h}^{n,j-1}\bigr)+\delta_{2}^{n}\,.$ (4.102) We embark upon the proof by deriving suitable estimates for $\varepsilon^{n,1}$ and $e^{n,1}$ . To this end,
putting $\Phi_{h}^{n}:=\Phi_{h}^{n,0}$ , we have $\Phi^{n}-\Phi_{h}^{n}=e^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}(H^{n}U^{n}-H_{h}^{n}U_{h}^{n})\,,$ and since $\displaystyle H^{n}U^{n}-H_{h}^{n}U_{h}^{n}$ $\displaystyle=H^{n}(U^{n}-U_{h}^{n})-(U^{n}-U_{h}^{n})(H^{n}-H_{h}^{n})+U^{n}(H^{n}-H_{h}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=H^{n}e^{n}-\varepsilon^{n}e^{n}+U^{n}\varepsilon^{n}\,,$ we will have $\Phi^{n}-\Phi_{h}^{n}=e^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}(H^{n}e^{n}-\varepsilon^{n}e^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}U^{n}\varepsilon^{n}\,,$ i.e. $\Phi^{n}-\Phi_{h}^{n}=\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}U^{n}\varepsilon^{n}\,,$ where $\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}^{n}=e^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}(H^{n}e^{n}-\varepsilon^{n}e^{n})\,.$ Therefore, $P(\Phi^{n}-\Phi_{h}^{n})_{x}=\gamma_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}\,,$ (4.103) where $\gamma_{1}^{n}=P\widetilde{\gamma}_{1x}^{n}\,,\qquad\quad\rho_{1}^{n}=P(U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}\,.$ (4.104) Hence, from $(\ref{eq499})$ $\varepsilon^{n,1}=\varepsilon^{n}-a_{1}k\gamma_{1}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}}{2}k\rho_{1}^{n}\,.$ (4.105) Let $0\leq n^{*}\leq M-1$ be the maximal integer for which $\|\varepsilon^{n}\|_{1}+\|e^{n}\|_{1}\leq 1\,,\qquad 0\leq n\leq n^{*}\,.$ Then, for $n\leq n^{*}$ , $\|\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,,$ and, in view of $(\ref{eq4104})$ , $\|\gamma_{1}^{n}\|\leq C(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ (4.106) Therefore, by $(\ref{eq499})$ , for $0\leq n\leq n^{*}$ , $\|\varepsilon^{n,1}\|\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,,$ (4.107) and also $\|\varepsilon^{n,1}\|_{1}\leq 1+C_{\lambda}\|\gamma_{1}^{n}\|+C_{\lambda}\|\rho_{1}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}\,,$ (4.108) where, as before, $C_{\lambda}$ denotes a constant that is a polynomial of $\lambda$ with positive
coefficients. In order to estimate $e^{n,1}$ we note, setting $F_{h}^{n}=F_{h}^{n,0}$ , that $F^{n}-F_{h}^{n}=\varepsilon_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{3}{2}(U^{n}U_{x}^{n}-U_{h}^{n}U_{hx}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{2}(H^{n}H_{x}^{n}-H_{h}^{n}H_{hx}^{n})\,.$ Since $\displaystyle U^{n}U_{x}^{n}-U_{h}^{n}U_{hx}^{n}$ $\displaystyle=U^{n}(U_{x}^{n}-U_{hx}^{n})-(U_{x}^{n}-U_{hx}^{n})(U^{n}-U_{h}^{n})+U_{x}^{n}(U^{n}-U_{h}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=(U^{n}e^{n})_{x}-e^{n}e_{x}^{n}\,,$ and $H^{n}H_{x}^{n}-H_{h}^{n}H_{hx}^{n}=(H^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}-\varepsilon^{n}\varepsilon_{x}^{n}\,,$ we have $F^{n}-F_{h}^{n}=\varepsilon_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{3}{2}(U^{n}e^{n})_{x}-\tfrac{3}{2}e^{n}e_{x}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{2}(H^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x}-\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{n}\varepsilon_{x}^{n}\,,$ and therefore, for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|F^{n}-F_{h}^{n}\|_{-1}\leq C(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|)\,.$ (4.109) Recalling the expression for $e^{n,1}$ in $(\ref{eq4101})$ we have for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|e^{n,1}\|_{1}\leq C(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ (4.110) We now turn to deriving estimates for $\varepsilon^{n,2}$ and $e^{n,2}$ . With this aim in mind, we note
for later reference that it follows from the definition of $V^{n,j}$ , $W^{n,j}$ , $F^{n,j}$ that for $0\leq n\leq M-1$ , $j=0,1,2,3$ $\|V^{n,j}\|_{1}+\|W^{n,j}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}\,,\qquad\|F^{n,j}\|_{-1}\leq C_{\lambda}\,.$ Since now $\Phi^{n,1}-\Phi_{h}^{n,1}=e^{n,1}+\tfrac{1}{2}(V^{n,1}W^{n,1}-H_{h}^{n,1}U_{h}^{n,1})\,,$ and $\displaystyle V^{n,1}W^{n,1}-H_{h}^{n,1}U_{h}^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle=V^{n,1}(W^{n,1}-U_{h}^{n,1})-(W^{n,1}-U_{h}^{n,1})(V^{n,1}-H_{h}^{n,1})+W^{n,1}(V^{n,1}-H_{h}^{n,1})$ $\displaystyle=V^{n,1}e^{n,1}-e^{n,1}\varepsilon^{n,1}+W^{n,1}\varepsilon^{n,1}\,,$ using $(\ref{eq480})$ we have by $(\ref{eq4105})$ $\displaystyle W^{n,1}\varepsilon^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle=(U^{n}-a_{1}kAF^{n})(\varepsilon^{n}-a_{1}k\gamma_{1}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=U^{n}\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}k}{2}U^{n}\rho_{1}^{n}-a_{1}kU^{n}\gamma_{1}^{n}-a_{1}k(AF^{n})\varepsilon^{n,1}\,,$ and so $\Phi^{n,1}-\Phi_{h}^{n,1}=\tfrac{1}{2}U^{n}\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}k}{4}U^{n}\rho_{1}^{n}+\widetilde{\gamma}_{2}^{n}\,,$ where $\widetilde{\gamma}_{2}^{n}=e^{n,1}+\tfrac{1}{2}V^{n,1}e^{n,1}-\tfrac{1}{2}e^{n,1}\varepsilon^{n,1}-\tfrac{a_{1}k}{2}U^{n}\gamma_{1}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}k}{2}(AF^{n})\varepsilon^{n,1}\,.$ (4.111) Recalling the definition of $\rho_{1}^{n}$ in $(\ref{eq4104})$ we obtain $P(\Phi^{n,1}-\Phi_{h}^{n,1})_{x}=\tfrac{1}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}k}{4}\rho_{2}^{n}+\gamma_{2}^{n}\,,$ (4.112) where $\gamma_{2}^{n}=P(\widetilde{\gamma}_{2}^{n})_{x}\,,\qquad\quad\rho_{2}^{n}=P(U^{n}\rho_{1}^{n})_{x}\,.$ (4.113) By $(\ref{eq4111})$ and $(\ref{eq4108})$ we deduce that $\|\widetilde{\gamma}_{2}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}\|e^{n,1}\|_{1}+Ck\|\gamma_{1}^{n}\|_{1}+C_{\lambda}k\|\varepsilon^{n,1}\|_{1}\,.$ Hence, by $(\ref{eq4110})$ and $(\ref{eq4106})$ , $(\ref{eq4107})$ we have $\|\widetilde{\gamma}_{2}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,,$ and therefore, by $(\ref{eq4113})$ we see that $\|\gamma_{2}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,,$ (4.114) holds for $n\leq n^{*}$ . Now, by $(\ref{eq480})$ and $(\ref{eq478})$ $\varepsilon^{n,2}=V^{n,2}-H_{h}^{n,2}=\varepsilon^{n}-a_{2}kP(\Phi^{n,1}-\Phi_{h}^{n,1})_{x}\,,$ and from $(\ref{eq4112})$ $\varepsilon^{n,2}=\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{a_{2}k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}}{4}\rho_{2}^{n}-a_{2}k\gamma_{2}^{n}\,.$ (4.115) Thus, $\|\varepsilon^{n,2}\|\leq\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+Ck\|\rho_{1}^{n}\|+Ck^{2}\|\rho_{2}^{n}\|+Ck\|\gamma_{2}^{n}\|\,,$ and using $(\ref{eq4113})$ , $(\ref{eq4104})$ and $(\ref{eq4114})$ we conclude that for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|\varepsilon^{n,2}\|\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,,$ (4.116) and $\|\varepsilon^{n,2}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}\,.$ (4.117) We next consider $e^{n,2}$ . Since $F^{n,1}-F_{h}^{n,1}=\varepsilon_{x}^{n,1}+\tfrac{3}{2}(W^{n,1}W_{x}^{n,1}-U_{h}^{n,1}U_{hx}^{n,1})+\tfrac{1}{2}(V^{n,1}V_{x}^{n,1}-H_{h}^{n,1}H_{hx}^{n,1})\,,$ $\displaystyle W^{n,1}W_{x}^{n,1}-U_{h}^{n,1}U_{hx}^{n,1}$ $\displaystyle=W^{n,1}(W_{x}^{n,1}-U_{hx}^{n,1})-(W_{x}^{n,1}-U_{hx}^{n,1})(W^{n,1}-U_{h}^{n,1})+W_{x}^{n,1}(W^{n,1}-U_{h}^{n,1})$ $\displaystyle=(W^{n,1}e^{n,1})_{x}-e_{x}^{n,1}e^{n,1}\,,$ and $V^{n,1}V_{x}^{n,1}-H_{h}^{n,1}H_{hx}^{n,1}=(V^{n,1}\varepsilon^{n,1})_{x}-\varepsilon_{x}^{n,1}\varepsilon^{n,1}\,,$ we obtain by $(\ref{eq4108})$ , for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|F^{n,1}-F_{h}^{n,1}\|_{-1}\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n,1}\|+\|e^{n,1}\|_{1})\,,$ giving, in view of $(\ref{eq4107})$ and $(\ref{eq4110})$ for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|F^{n,1}-F_{h}^{n,1}\|_{-1}\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ (4.118) Therefore, by $(\ref{eq4100})$ we conclude that for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|e^{n,2}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ (4.119) To obtain analogous bounds for $\varepsilon^{n,3}$ , $e^{n,3}$ note that $\Phi^{n,2}-\Phi_{h}^{n,2}=e^{n,2}+\tfrac{1}{2}(V^{n,2}W^{n,2}-H_{h}^{n,2}U_{h}^{n,2})\,,$ and $\displaystyle V^{n,2}W^{n,2}-H_{h}^{n,2}U_{h}^{n,2}$ $\displaystyle=V^{n,2}(W^{n,2}-U_{h}^{n,2})-(W^{n,2}-U_{h}^{n,2})(V^{n,2}-H_{h}^{n,2})+W^{n,2}(V^{n,2}-H_{h}^{n,2})$ $\displaystyle=V^{n,2}e^{n,2}-e^{n,2}\varepsilon^{n,2}+W^{n,2}\varepsilon^{n,2}\,.$ By $(\ref{eq480})$ and $(\ref{eq4115})$ we see that $\displaystyle W^{n,2}\varepsilon^{n,2}$ $\displaystyle=(U^{n}-a_{2}kAF^{n,1})(\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{a_{2}k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}}{4}\rho_{2}^{n}-a_{2}k\gamma_{2}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=U^{n}\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{a_{2}k}{2}U^{n}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}}{4}U^{n}\rho_{2}^{n}-a_{2}kU^{n}\gamma_{2}^{n}-a_{2}k(AF^{n,1})\varepsilon^{n,2}\,.$ Therefore $\Phi^{n,2}-\Phi_{h}^{n,2}=\tfrac{1}{2}U^{n}\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{a_{2}k}{4}U^{n}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}}{8}U^{n}\rho_{2}^{n}+\widetilde{\gamma}_{3}^{n}\,,$ where $\widetilde{\gamma}_{3}^{n}=e^{n,2}+\tfrac{1}{2}V^{n,2}e^{n,2}-\tfrac{1}{2}e^{n,2}\varepsilon^{n,2}-\tfrac{a_{2}k}{2}U^{n}\gamma_{2}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{2}k}{2}(AF^{n,1})\varepsilon^{n,2}\,.$ (4.120) So, by $(\ref{eq4104})$ and $(\ref{eq4113})$ we have $P(\Phi^{n,2}-\Phi_{h}^{n,2})_{x}=\tfrac{1}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{2}k}{4}\rho_{2}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}k^{2}}{8}\rho_{3}^{n}+\gamma_{3}^{n}\,,$ (4.121) where $\gamma_{3}^{n}=P(\widetilde{\gamma}_{3}^{n})_{x}\,,\qquad\quad\rho_{3}^{n}=P(U^{n}\rho_{2}^{n})_{x}\,.$ (4.122) Now, by $(\ref{eq4120})$ and $(\ref{eq4117})$ we have for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|\widetilde{\gamma}_{3}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}\|e^{n,2}\|_{1}+Ck\|\gamma_{2}^{n}\|_{1}+C_{\lambda}k\|\varepsilon^{n,2}\|_{1}\,.$ Hence, by $(\ref{eq4119})$ , $(\ref{eq4114})$ and $(\ref{eq4116})$ , $\|\widetilde{\gamma}_{3}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,,$ and thus $\|\gamma_{3}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,,$ (4.123) for $n\leq n^{*}$ . Now $\varepsilon^{n,3}=V^{n,3}-H_{h}^{n,3}=\varepsilon^{n}-kP(\Phi^{n,2}-\Phi_{h}^{n,2})_{x}\,,$ and from $(\ref{eq4121})$ $\varepsilon^{n,3}=\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{2}k^{2}}{4}\rho_{2}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}}{8}\rho_{3}^{n}-k\gamma_{3}^{n}\,.$ (4.124) Therefore, $\|\varepsilon^{n,3}\|\leq\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+Ck\|\rho_{1}^{n}\|+Ck^{2}\|\rho_{2}^{n}\|+Ck^{3}\|\rho_{3}^{n}\|+Ck\|\gamma_{3}^{n}\|\,,$ and by $(\ref{eq4122})$ , $(\ref{eq4113})$ , $(\ref{eq4104})$ and $(\ref{eq4123})$ we deduce that
for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|\varepsilon^{n,3}\|\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,,$ (4.125) and also $\|\varepsilon^{n,3}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}\,.$ (4.126) We consider now $e^{n,3}$ . Since $F^{n,2}-F_{h}^{n,2}=\varepsilon_{x}^{n,2}+\tfrac{3}{2}(W^{n,2}W_{x}^{n,2}-U_{h}^{n,2}U_{hx}^{n,2})+\tfrac{1}{2}(V^{n,2}V_{x}^{n,2}-H_{h}^{n,2}H_{hx}^{n,2})\,,$ $\displaystyle W^{n,2}W_{x}^{n,2}-U_{h}^{n,2}U_{hx}^{n,2}$ $\displaystyle=W^{n,2}(W_{x}^{n,2}-U_{hx}^{n,2})-(W_{x}^{n,2}-U_{hx}^{n,2})(W^{n,2}-U_{h}^{n,2})+W_{x}^{n,2}(W^{n,2}-U_{h}^{n,2})$ $\displaystyle=(W^{n,2}e^{n,2})_{x}-e_{x}^{n,2}e^{n,2}\,,$ and $V^{n,2}V_{x}^{n,2}-H_{h}^{n,2}H_{hx}^{n,2}=(V^{n,2}\varepsilon^{n,2})_{x}-\varepsilon_{x}^{n,2}\varepsilon^{n,2}\,,$ we have by $(\ref{eq4117})$ for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|F^{n,2}-F_{h}^{n,2}\|_{-1}\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n,2}\|+\|e^{n,2}\|_{1})\,,$ and from $(\ref{eq4116})$ and $(\ref{eq4119})$ , for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|F^{n,2}-F_{h}^{n,2}\|_{-1}\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ (4.127) Therefore, we conclude by $(\ref{eq4100})$ , for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|e^{n,3}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ (4.128) We have now reached the final stage of the estimation of the errors of the fully discrete scheme.
First, we estimate $e^{n+1}$ :
Since $F^{n,3}-F_{h}^{n,3}=\varepsilon_{x}^{n,3}+\tfrac{3}{2}(W^{n,3}W_{x}^{n,3}-U_{h}^{n,3}U_{hx}^{n,3})+\tfrac{1}{2}(V^{n,3}V_{x}^{n,3}-H_{h}^{n,3}H_{hx}^{n,3})\,,$ $W^{n,3}W_{x}^{n,3}-U_{h}^{n,3}U_{hx}^{n,3}=(W^{n,3}e^{n,3})_{x}-e_{x}^{n,3}e^{n,3}\,,$ and $V^{n,3}V_{x}^{n,3}-H_{h}^{n,3}H_{hx}^{n,3}=(V^{n,3}\varepsilon^{n,3})_{x}-\varepsilon_{x}^{n,3}\varepsilon^{n,3}\,,$ we have from $(\ref{eq4126})$ for $n\leq n^{*}$ , $\|F^{n,3}-F_{h}^{n,3}\|_{-1}\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n,3}\|+\|e^{n,3}\|_{1})\,,$ and therefore, from $(\ref{eq4125})$ , $(\ref{eq4128})$ $\|F^{n,3}-F_{h}^{n,3}\|_{-1}\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ Thus, by $(\ref{eq4102})$ , $(\ref{eq4109})$ , $(\ref{eq4118})$ , $(\ref{eq4127})$ , and above
inequality, we obtain for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|e^{n+1}\|_{1}\leq\|e^{n}\|_{1}+C_{\lambda}k(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})+\|\delta_{2}^{n}\|_{1}\,,$ (4.129) which is the required recursive inequality for $\|e^{n+1}\|_{1}$ .
To obtain an analogous relation for $\|\varepsilon^{n+1}\|$ requires more work. Observe that $\Phi^{n,3}-\Phi_{h}^{n,3}=e^{n,3}+\tfrac{1}{2}(V^{n,3}W^{n,3}-H_{h}^{n,3}U_{h}^{n,3})\,,$ and $\displaystyle V^{n,3}W^{n,3}-H_{h}^{n,3}U_{h}^{n,3}$ $\displaystyle=V^{n,3}(W^{n,3}-U_{h}^{n,3})-(W^{n,3}-U_{h}^{n,3})(V^{n,3}-H_{h}^{n,3})+W^{n,3}(V^{n,3}-H_{h}^{n,3})$ $\displaystyle=V^{n,3}e^{n,3}-e^{n,3}\varepsilon^{n,3}+W^{n,3}\varepsilon^{n,3}\,.$ By $(\ref{eq480})$ and $(\ref{eq4124})$ we obtain $\displaystyle W^{n,3}\varepsilon^{n,3}$ $\displaystyle=(U^{n}-kAF^{n,2})(\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{2}k^{2}}{4}\rho_{2}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}}{8}\rho_{3}^{n}-k\gamma_{3}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=U^{n}\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}U^{n}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{2}k^{2}}{4}U^{n}\rho_{2}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}}{8}U^{n}\rho_{3}^{n}-kU^{n}\gamma_{3}^{n}-k(AF^{n,2})\varepsilon^{n,3}\,.$ Thus $\Phi^{n,3}-\Phi_{h}^{n,3}=\tfrac{1}{2}U^{n}\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{4}U^{n}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{2}k^{2}}{8}U^{n}\rho_{2}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}}{16}U^{n}\rho_{3}^{n}+\widetilde{\gamma}_{4}^{n}\,,$ where $\widetilde{\gamma}_{4}^{n}=e^{n,3}+\tfrac{1}{2}V^{n,3}e^{n,3}-\tfrac{1}{2}e^{n,3}\varepsilon^{n,3}-\tfrac{k}{2}U^{n}\gamma_{3}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}(AF^{n,2})\varepsilon^{n,3}\,.$ (4.130) Hence, by $(\ref{eq4104})$ , $(\ref{eq4113})$ , and $(\ref{eq4132})$ we obtain $P(\Phi^{n,3}-\Phi_{h}^{n,3})_{x}=\tfrac{1}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{4}\rho_{2}^{n}+\tfrac{a_{2}k^{2}}{8}\rho_{3}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}}{16}\rho_{4}^{n}+\gamma_{4}^{n}\,,$ (4.131) where $\gamma_{4}^{n}=P(\widetilde{\gamma}_{4}^{n})_{x}\,,\qquad\quad\rho_{4}^{n}=P(U^{n}\rho_{3}^{n})_{x}\,.$ (4.132) By $(\ref{eq4130})$ and $(\ref{eq4126})$ we obtain for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|\widetilde{\gamma}_{4}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}\|e^{n,3}\|_{1}+Ck\|\gamma_{3}^{n}\|_{1}+C_{\lambda}k\|\varepsilon^{n,3}\|_{1}\,,$ from which, using $(\ref{eq4128})$ , $(\ref{eq4123})$ and $(\ref{eq4125})$ we see that for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|\widetilde{\gamma}_{4}^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,,$ and $\|\gamma_{4}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,.$ (4.133) From $(\ref{eq4103})$ and $(\ref{eq4112})$ we obtain $\displaystyle b_{1}P(\Phi^{n}-\Phi_{h}^{n})_{x}+b_{2}P(\Phi^{n,1}-\Phi_{h}^{n,1})_{x}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(b_{1}+b_{2})\rho_{1}^{n}-\tfrac{a_{1}b_{2}k}{4}\rho_{2}^{n}+b_{1}\gamma_{1}+b_{2}\gamma_{2}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{4}\rho_{1}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{24}\rho_{2}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{6}\gamma_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{3}\gamma_{2}^{n}\,.$ From $(\ref{eq4121})$ and $(\ref{eq4131})$ we see that $\displaystyle b_{3}P(\Phi^{n,2}-\Phi_{h}^{n,2})_{x}+b_{4}P(\Phi^{n,3}-\Phi_{h}^{n,3})_{x}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(b_{3}+b_{4})\rho_{1}^{n}-\tfrac{(b_{3}a_{2}+b_{4})k}{4}\rho_{2}^{n}+\tfrac{(b_{3}a_{1}a_{2}+b_{4}a_{2})k^{2}}{8}\rho_{3}^{n}$ $\displaystyle\qquad-\tfrac{b_{4}a_{1}a_{2}k^{3}}{16}\rho_{4}^{n}+b_{3}\gamma_{3}^{n}+b_{4}\gamma_{4}^{n}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{4}\rho_{1}^{n}-\tfrac{k}{12}\rho_{2}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{48}\rho_{3}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{3}}{24\cdot 16}\rho_{4}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{3}\gamma_{3}^{n}+\tfrac{1}{6}\gamma_{4}^{n}\,.$ Therefore, in view of $(\ref{eq4101})$ , we have $\varepsilon^{n+1}=\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{8}\rho_{2}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{3}}{48}\rho_{3}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{4}}{24\cdot 16}\rho_{4}^{n}-k\gamma_{5}^{n}+\delta_{1}^{n}\,,$ (4.134) where $\gamma_{5}^{n}=\tfrac{1}{6}(\gamma_{1}^{n}+\gamma_{4}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{3}(\gamma_{2}^{n}+\gamma_{3}^{n})\,,$ satisfies, in view of $(\ref{eq4106})$ , $(\ref{eq4114})$ , $(\ref{eq4123})$ and $(\ref{eq4133})$ ,
the inequality $\|\gamma_{5}^{n}\|\leq C_{\lambda}(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})\,,$ (4.135) for $n\leq n^{*}$ . We begin now the estimation of $\sigma^{n}:=\varepsilon^{n}-\tfrac{k}{2}\rho_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{8}\rho_{2}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{3}}{48}\rho_{3}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{4}}{24\cdot 16}\rho_{4}^{n}\,.$ (4.136) We have $\displaystyle\|\sigma^{n}\|^{2}$ $\displaystyle=\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}-k(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})+\tfrac{k^{2}}{4}\bigl[\|\rho_{1}^{n}\|^{2}+(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})\bigr]-\tfrac{k^{3}}{24}\bigl[(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})+3(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})\bigr]$ $\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{k^{4}}{64}\bigl[\|\rho_{2}^{n}\|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{3}(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{4}^{n})+\tfrac{4}{3}(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})\bigr]-\tfrac{k^{5}}{24\cdot 16}\bigl[(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{4}^{n})+2(\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})\bigr]$ $\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{k^{6}}{48^{2}}\bigl[\|\rho_{3}^{n}\|^{2}+\tfrac{3}{2}(\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{4}^{n})\bigr]-\tfrac{k^{7}}{24^{2}\cdot 16}(\rho_{3}^{n},\rho_{4}^{n})+\tfrac{k^{8}}{24^{2}\cdot 16^{2}}\|\rho_{4}^{n}\|^{2}\,,$ or $\|\sigma^{n}\|^{2}=\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}-kf_{1}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{2}}{4}f_{2}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{3}}{24}f_{3}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{4}}{64}f_{4}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{5}}{24\cdot 16}f_{5}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{6}}{48^{2}}f_{6}^{n}-\tfrac{k^{7}}{24^{2}\cdot 16}f_{7}^{n}+\tfrac{k^{8}}{24^{2}\cdot 16^{2}}\|\rho_{4}^{n}\|^{2}\,,$ (4.137) where the numbers $f_{1}^{n},\dots,f_{7}^{n}$ have been defined in the obvious manner.
By $(\ref{eq4104})$ we have $f_{1}^{n}=(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})=-(U^{n}\varepsilon_{x}^{n},\varepsilon^{n})=\tfrac{1}{2}(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\varepsilon^{n})\,,$ and therefore $|f_{1}^{n}|\leq C\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\,.$ (4.138) By $(\ref{eq4113})$ and $(\ref{eq4104})$ $(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})=-(U^{n}\varepsilon_{x}^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})=-((U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x},\rho_{1}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})=-\|\rho_{1}^{n}\|^{2}+(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})\,.$ Since $f_{2}^{n}=\|\rho_{1}^{n}\|^{2}+(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})\,,$ we conclude that $f_{2}^{n}=(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})\leq\tfrac{C}{h}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\,.$ (4.139) Now $(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})=\tfrac{1}{2}(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})\,.$ In addition, using $(\ref{eq4122})$ , $(\ref{eq4104})$ , and $(\ref{eq4113})$ we have $(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})=-(U^{n}\varepsilon_{x}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})=-(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})=-\tfrac{1}{2}(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})\,.$ Hence, since $f_{3}^{n}=(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})+3(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})\,,$ we have $f_{3}^{n}=(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{1}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})\,,$ from which $|f_{3}^{n}|\leq\tfrac{C}{h^{2}}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\,.$ (4.140) Now $(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})=-(U^{n}\rho_{1x}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})=-((U^{n}\rho_{1}^{n})_{x},\rho_{2}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})=-\|\rho_{2}^{n}\|^{2}+(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})\,,$ and by $(\ref{eq4132})$ $\displaystyle(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{4}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=-(U^{n}\varepsilon_{x}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})=-((U^{n}\varepsilon^{n})_{x},\rho_{3}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})=-(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=\|\rho_{2}^{n}\|^{2}-(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})\,.$ So, since $f_{4}^{n}=\|\rho_{2}^{n}\|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{3}(\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{4}^{n})+\tfrac{4}{3}(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})\,,$ we have $f_{4}^{n}=(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})+\tfrac{1}{3}(U_{x}^{n}\varepsilon^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})\,,$ and thus $|f_{4}^{n}|\leq\tfrac{C}{h^{3}}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\,.$ (4.141) We also have $(\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})=\tfrac{1}{2}(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})\,,$ and $\displaystyle(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{4}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=-(U^{n}\rho_{1x}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})=-((U^{n}\rho_{1}^{n})_{x},\rho_{3}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})=-(\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})$ $\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{2}(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})\,.$ Since $f_{5}^{n}=(\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{4}^{n})+2(\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})\,,$ we have $f_{5}^{n}=\tfrac{1}{2}(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{2}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{1}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})\,,$ and therefore $|f_{5}^{n}|\leq\tfrac{C}{h^{4}}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\,.$ (4.142) Similarly, $(\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{4}^{n})=-(U^{n}\rho_{2x}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})=-((U^{n}\rho_{2}^{n})_{x},\rho_{3}^{n})+(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})=-\|\rho_{3}^{n}\|^{2}+(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})\,.$ Since $f_{6}^{n}=\|\rho_{3}^{n}\|^{2}+\tfrac{3}{2}(\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{4}^{n})\,,$ we have $f_{6}^{n}=\tfrac{3}{2}(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{2}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})-\tfrac{1}{2}\|\rho_{3}^{n}\|^{2}\leq\tfrac{C}{h^{5}}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\|\rho_{3}^{n}\|^{2}\,.$ (4.143) Finally, $f_{7}^{n}=(\rho_{3}^{n},\rho_{4}^{n})=\tfrac{1}{2}(U_{x}^{n}\rho_{3}^{n},\rho_{3}^{n})\,,$ and thus $|f_{7}^{n}|\leq\tfrac{C}{h^{6}}\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}\,.$ (4.144) Now, from the inequalities $(\ref{eq4138})$ - $(\ref{eq4144})$ and the fact that $\|\rho_{4}^{n}\|\leq\tfrac{\widetilde{C}}{h}\|\rho_{3}^{n}\|\,,$ where $\widetilde{C}$ is a constant independent of $k$ and $h$ , we obtain in $(\ref{eq4137})$ $\|\sigma^{n}\|^{2}\leq(1+C_{\lambda}k)\|\varepsilon^{n}\|^{2}+\tfrac{k^{6}}{2\cdot 48^{2}}(\tfrac{\widetilde{C}\lambda^{2}}{32}-1)\|\rho_{3}^{n}\|^{2}\,.$ Thus, for $\lambda\leq\lambda_{0}=\sqrt{32/\widetilde{C}}$ we will have the following estimate of $\|\sigma^{n}\|$ $\|\sigma^{n}\|\leq(1+C_{\lambda}k)\|\varepsilon^{n}\|\,.$ Therefore, from $(\ref{eq4134})$ , $(\ref{eq4136})$ and $(\ref{eq4135})$ , we finally obtain $\|\varepsilon^{n+1}\|\leq\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+C_{\lambda}k(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})+\|\delta_{1}^{n}\|\,,$ for $n=0,1,\dots,n^{*}$ . From this inequality, $(\ref{eq4129})$ and Lemma $4.5$ we obtain
for $n\leq n^{*}$ $\|\varepsilon^{n+1}\|+\|e^{n+1}\|_{1}\leq(1+C_{\lambda}k)(\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1})+Ck(k^{4}+h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h})\,.$ Therefore, by Gronwall’s Lemma, and $(\ref{eq469})$ we see that $\|\varepsilon^{n}\|+\|e^{n}\|_{1}\leq C_{\lambda}(k^{4}+h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h})\,,$ for $n=0,1,\dots,n^{*}$ . Hence, if $k$ and $h$ are sufficiently small, the maximality of $n^{*}$ contradicted and we may take $n^{*}=M-1$ . The conclusion of the proposition follows.
∎ Remark 4.5 . Arguing as in Remark $4.1$ we have again $\|U_{h}^{n}-u(t^{n})\|_{\infty}=O(k^{4}+h^{3.5}\sqrt{\ln 1/h})$ . Remark 4.6 . The analogous results of Lemma $4.5$ of Proposition $4.3$ hold for the system $(\ref{cb})$ as well. 5. A non-standard Galerkin method In this section we analyze a semidiscrete Galerkin approximation of $(\ref{cb})$ and $(\ref{scb})$ which is non-standard in the sense that it approximates $\eta$ by piecewise linear, continuous
functions and $u$ by piecewise quadratic, $C_{0}^{1}$ functions, defined on the same mesh. (The method
may be generalized using the analogous pairs of higher-order splines but here we confine ourselves to the
low-order case.) In the analysis, we consider only the uniform mesh case, where a series of
superaccuracy results for the error of the $L^{2}$ -projection onto the space of piecewise linear
continuous functions affords proving optimal-order error estimates. 5.1. Preliminaries and the basic results Using the notation of paragraph $2.2$ ,
in addition to $S_{h}^{2}$ we let $S_{h,0}^{3}:=\{\phi\in C^{1}:\phi\big|_{[x_{j},x_{j+1}]}\in\mathbb{P}_{2}\,,\,\,1\leq j\leq N\,,\,\,\phi(0)=\phi(1)=0\}\,.$ In this section we shall denote by $a(\cdot,\cdot)$ the usual bilinear form $a(\psi,\chi)=(\psi,\chi)+\tfrac{1}{3}(\psi^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})$ defined for $\psi$ , $\chi\in S_{h,0}^{3}$ and let $R_{h}:H_{0}^{1}\to S_{h,0}^{3}$ be the elliptic projection operator defined for $v\in H_{0}^{1}$ by $((R_{h}v)^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})=(v^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})$ $\forall\chi\in S_{h,0}^{3}$ . The system $(\ref{scb})$ is
discretized as follows: Seek $\eta_{h}:[0,T]\to S_{h}^{2}$ , $u_{h}:[0,T]\to S_{h,0}^{3}$ such that
for $0\leq t\leq T$ $\displaystyle(\eta_{ht},\phi)+(u_{hx},\phi)+\tfrac{1}{2}((\eta_{h}u_{h})_{x},\phi)$ $\displaystyle=0\,,\quad\forall\phi\in S_{h}^{2}$ (5.1) $\displaystyle a(u_{ht},\chi)+(\eta_{hx},\chi)+\tfrac{3}{2}(u_{h}u_{hx},\chi)+\tfrac{1}{2}(\eta_{h}\eta_{hx},\chi)$ $\displaystyle=0\,,\quad\forall\chi\in S_{h,0}^{3}$ (5.2) and $\eta_{h}(0)=P\eta_{0}\,,\qquad u_{h}(0)=R_{h}u_{0}\,,$ (5.3) where $P:L^{2}\to S_{h}^{2}$ is the $L^{2}$ -projection operator onto $S_{h}^{2}$ . (We will treat
only the case of $(\ref{scb})$ , that of $(\ref{cb})$ being similar.) The o.d.e. initial-value problem
represented by $(\ref{eq51})$ - $(\ref{eq53})$ has a unique solution for any $T<\infty$ . In fact,
taking $\phi=\eta_{h}$ in $(\ref{eq51})$ and $\chi=u_{h}$ in $(\ref{eq52})$ and adding the resulting
equations, we obtain for $t\geq 0$ $\|\eta_{h}(t)\|^{2}+\|u_{h}(t)\|_{1}^{2}=\|\eta_{h}(0)\|^{2}+\|u_{h}(0)\|_{1}^{2}\,,$ (5.4) and the desired conclusion follows by standard o.d.e. theory. The plan of the section is as follows: We will start by stating the preliminary Lemmas $5.1$ - $5.4$ with the aid of which we will prove our main error estimate, Theorem $5.1$ . The proofs of the Lemmas
depend on some superaccuracy estimates of the $L^{2}$ projection error $v-Pv$ for smooth
functions $v$ ; these are proved in § $5.2$ . (Lemmas $5.5$ - $5.10$ ). Recall first the following standard
estimates, [ 19 ] , [ 30 ] . Suppose that $\eta\in C^{2}$ and $u\in C_{0}^{3}$ . Then, if $\rho=\eta-P\eta$ , $\sigma=u-R_{h}u$ , we have $\|\rho\|+h\|\rho\|_{1}\leq Ch^{2}\,,\quad\|\sigma\|+h\|\sigma\|_{1}\leq Ch^{3}\,,\quad\|\sigma\|_{\infty}+h\|\rho\|_{\infty}\leq Ch^{3}\,.$ Lemma 5.1 . Let $\eta\in C^{4}$ , $v\in C_{0}^{2}$ . If $\rho=\eta-P\eta$ and $\zeta\in S_{h}^{2}$ is such that $(\zeta,\phi)=((v\rho)^{\prime},\phi)\quad\forall\phi\in S_{h}^{2}\,,$ then $\|\zeta\|\leq Ch^{3}$ . Proof. The result follows from Lemma $5.9$ and Lemma $2.1(ii)$ .
∎ Lemma 5.2 . Let $\eta\in C^{2}$ and $v\in C_{0}^{1}$ . If $\rho=\eta-P\eta$ and $\zeta\in S_{h}^{2}$ is such that $(\zeta,\phi)=(v\rho,\phi)\quad\forall\phi\in S_{h}^{2}\,,$ then $\|\zeta\|\leq Ch^{3}$ . Proof. The result follows from Lemma $5.10$ and Lemma $2.1(ii)$ .
∎ Lemma 5.3 . Let $\eta\in C^{2}$ and $u\in C_{0}^{3}$ . If $\rho=\eta-P\eta$ , $\sigma=u-R_{h}u$ , then (i) $(\rho^{\prime},\psi)=0\quad\forall\psi\in S_{h,0}^{3}$ (ii) $(\sigma^{\prime},\phi)=0\quad\forall\phi\in S_{h}^{2}$ . Proof. The identity $(i)$ follows from the observation that $(\rho^{\prime},\psi)=(\rho,\psi^{\prime})=0$ , since $\psi\in S_{h,0}^{3}$ implies $\psi^{\prime}\in S_{h}^{2}$ .
In order to prove $(ii)$ , we use an argument similar to that of Wahlbin, $[30$ ,p $.5]$ . Let $\{\phi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N+1}$ be the usual hat function basis of $S_{h}^{2}$ associated with the
uniform mesh $x_{i}=(i-1)h$ , $1\leq i\leq N+1$ , i.e. with the property that $\phi_{i}(x_{j})=\delta_{ij}$ . Then, the function $\psi_{i}$ defined for $1\leq i\leq N+1$ on $[0,1]$ by $\psi_{i}(x)=\int_{0}^{x}\phi_{i}d\xi-x\int_{0}^{1}\phi_{i}d\xi$ belongs to $S_{h,0}^{3}$ and
satisfies $\psi_{i}^{\prime}=\phi_{i}-\int_{0}^{1}\phi_{i}d\xi$ . Since $(\sigma^{\prime},1)=0$ , it follows that $(\sigma^{\prime},\phi_{i})=(\sigma^{\prime},\psi_{i}^{\prime})=0$ . Hence, $(\sigma^{\prime},\phi)=0$ for any $\phi\in S_{h}^{2}$ and the proof of $(ii)$ is completed. (Since $Pu^{\prime}=(R_{h}u)^{\prime}$ as it may be easily established, $(ii)$ implies that $\sigma^{\prime}=u^{\prime}-Pu^{\prime}$ .)
∎ Lemma 5.4 . Let $\eta\in C^{1}$ and $u\in C_{0}^{3}$ . If $\sigma=u-R_{h}u$ and $\zeta\in S_{h}^{2}$ is such that $(\zeta,\phi)=((\eta\sigma)^{\prime},\phi)\quad\forall\phi\in S_{h}^{2}\,,$ then $\|\zeta\|\leq Ch^{3}$ . Proof. Consider $\gamma_{i}=((\eta\sigma)^{\prime},\phi_{i})$ . Then, by the final remark in the proof
of Lemma $5.3$ , $\gamma_{i}=(\eta^{\prime}\sigma,\phi_{i})+(\eta\sigma^{\prime},\phi_{i})=(\eta^{\prime}\sigma,\phi_{i})+(\eta(u^{\prime}-Pu^{\prime}),\phi_{i})\,.$ Now, since $\|\sigma\|_{\infty}\leq Ch^{3}$ , we have that $|(\eta^{\prime}\sigma,\phi_{i})|\leq C\|\sigma\|_{\infty}\int_{0}^{1}\phi_{i}dx\leq Ch^{4}$ . In addition,
by Lemma $5.10$ we obtain that $|(\eta(u^{\prime}-Pu^{\prime}),\phi_{i})|\leq Ch^{4}$ . We conclude that $|\gamma|=(\sum_{i=1}^{N+1}\gamma_{i}^{2})^{1/2}\leq Ch^{3.5}$ , from which the conclusion of the
Lemma follows in view of Lemma $2.1(ii)$ .
∎ With the aid of these lemmas we may establish the basic result of this section: Theorem 5.1 . Suppose that the solution $(\eta,u)$ of $(\ref{scb})$ is such that $\eta\in C(0,T;C^{4})$ , $\eta_{t}\in C(0,T;C^{2})$ , $u$ , $u_{t}\in C(0,T;C_{0}^{3})$ and let $(\eta_{h},u_{h})$ be the solution
of the semidiscrete problem $(\ref{eq51})$ - $(\ref{eq53})$ . Then $\displaystyle(i)\,\,\,\,\,$ $\displaystyle\max_{0\leq t\leq T}(\|u(t)-u_{h}(t)\|+h\|\eta(t)-\eta_{h}(t)\|)\leq Ch^{3}\,,$ $\displaystyle(ii)\,\,\,\,\,$ $\displaystyle\max_{0\leq t\leq T}(\|u(t)-u_{h}(t)\|_{\infty}+h\|\eta(t)-\eta_{h}(t)\|_{\infty})\leq Ch^{3}\,.$ Proof. Let $\rho=\eta-P\eta$ , $\theta=P\eta-\eta_{h}$ , $\sigma=u-R_{h}u$ , and $\xi=R_{h}u-u_{h}$ . Then $\eta-\eta_{h}=\rho+\theta$ , $u-u_{h}=\sigma+\xi$ and, by Lemma $5.3$ $\displaystyle(\theta_{t},\phi)+(\xi_{x},\phi)+\tfrac{1}{2}((\eta u-\eta_{h}u_{h})_{x},\phi)$ $\displaystyle=0\quad\forall\phi\in S_{h}^{2}\,,$ (5.5) $\displaystyle a(\xi_{t},\psi)+(\theta_{x},\psi)+\tfrac{3}{2}(uu_{x}-u_{h}u_{hx},\psi)+\tfrac{1}{2}(\eta\eta_{x}-\eta_{h}\eta_{hx},\psi)$ $\displaystyle=-(\sigma_{t},\psi)\quad\forall\psi\in S_{h,0}^{3}\,,$ (5.6) for $t\in[0,T]$ . Since $\eta u-\eta_{h}u_{h}=\eta(u-u_{h})+u(\eta-\eta_{h})-(\eta-\eta_{h})(u-u_{h})=\eta(\sigma+\xi)+u(\rho+\theta)-(\rho+\theta)(\sigma+\xi)\,,$ it follows that $\eta u-\eta_{h}u_{h}=\eta\sigma+u\rho-\theta\xi+f\,,$ where $f=\eta\xi+u\theta-\rho\sigma-\rho\xi-\theta\sigma$ . In addition, $\displaystyle\eta\eta_{x}-\eta_{h}\eta_{hx}$ $\displaystyle=\eta(\eta_{x}-\eta_{hx})+\eta_{x}(\eta-\eta_{h})-(\eta-\eta_{h})(\eta_{x}-\eta_{hx})$ $\displaystyle=\eta(\rho_{x}+\theta_{x})+\eta_{x}(\rho+\theta)-(\rho+\theta)(\rho_{x}+\theta_{x})$ $\displaystyle=(\eta\rho)_{x}+(\eta\theta)_{x}-(\rho\theta)_{x}-\rho\rho_{x}-\theta\theta_{x}\,,$ whence $\eta\eta_{x}-\eta_{h}\eta_{hx}=(\eta\rho)_{x}-\theta\theta_{x}+g_{x}\,,$ where $g=\eta\theta-\rho\theta-\tfrac{1}{2}\rho^{2}$ . Finally, $\displaystyle uu_{x}-u_{h}u_{hx}$ $\displaystyle=u(u_{x}-u_{hx})+u_{x}(u-u_{h})-(u-u_{h})(u_{x}-u_{hx})$ $\displaystyle=u(\sigma_{x}+\xi_{x})+u_{x}(\sigma+\xi)-(\sigma+\xi)(\sigma_{x}+\xi_{x})$ $\displaystyle=(u\sigma)_{x}+(u\xi)_{x}-(\sigma\xi)_{x}-\sigma\sigma_{x}-\xi\xi_{x}\,,$ i.e. $uu_{x}-u_{h}u_{hx}=\hat{f}_{x}\,,$ where $\hat{f}=u\sigma+u\xi-\sigma\xi-\tfrac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\xi^{2}$ .
Thus we rewrite $(\ref{eq55})$ and $(\ref{eq56})$ as $\displaystyle(\theta_{t},\phi)+(\xi_{x},\phi)+\tfrac{1}{2}((\eta\sigma)_{x},\phi)+\tfrac{1}{2}((u\rho)_{x},\phi)-\tfrac{1}{2}((\theta\xi)_{x},\phi)+\tfrac{1}{2}(f_{x},\phi)$ $\displaystyle=0\quad\forall\phi\in S_{h}^{2}\,,$ $\displaystyle a(\xi_{t},\psi)+(\theta_{x},\psi)+\tfrac{3}{2}(\hat{f}_{x},\psi)+\tfrac{1}{2}((\eta\rho)_{x},\psi)-\tfrac{1}{2}((\theta\theta)_{x},\psi)+\tfrac{1}{2}(g_{x},\psi)$ $\displaystyle=-(\sigma_{t},\psi)\quad\forall\psi\in S_{h,0}^{3}\,.$ Putting $\phi=\theta$ , $\chi=\xi$ in the above and adding the resulting equations, we have for $0\leq t\leq T$ $\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{d}{dt}(\|\theta\|^{2}+\|\xi\|_{1}^{2})$ $\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}((\eta\sigma)_{x},\theta)+\tfrac{1}{2}((u\rho)_{x},\theta)+\tfrac{1}{2}(f_{x},\theta)$ (5.7) $\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}((\eta\rho)_{x},\xi)+\tfrac{3}{2}(\hat{f}_{x},\xi)+\tfrac{1}{2}(g_{x},\xi)=-(\sigma_{t},\xi)\,.$ We estimate the various terms in $(\ref{eq57})$ as follows. By Lemma $5.4$ we have $|((\eta\sigma)_{x},\theta)|\leq Ch^{3}\|\theta\|\,,$ and by Lemma $5.1$ $|((u\rho)_{x},\theta)|\leq Ch^{3}\|\theta\|\,.$ Using integration by parts and the standard estimates for $\rho$ and $\sigma$ we also have $|(f_{x},\theta)|\leq C\|\xi\|_{1}\|\theta\|+Ch^{4}\|\theta\|+C\|\theta\|^{2}\,.$ In addition, from Lemma $5.2$ , since $\xi_{x}\in S_{h}^{2}$ $|((\eta\rho)_{x},\xi)|=|(\eta\rho,\xi_{x})|\leq Ch^{3}\|\xi\|_{1}\,.$ Finally, it follows from integration by parts and standard estimates for $\sigma$ that $|(\hat{f}_{x},\xi)|=|(\hat{f},\xi_{x})|\leq Ch^{3}\|\xi\|_{1}+C\|\xi\|^{2}\,,$ and by standard estimates for $\rho$ that $|(g_{x},\xi)|=|(g,\xi_{x})|\leq C\|\theta\|\|\xi\|_{1}+Ch^{4}\|\xi\|_{1}\,.$ Hence, from $(\ref{eq57})$ and these estimates we deduce for $0\leq t\leq T$ $\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{d}{dt}(\|\theta\|^{2}+\|\xi\|_{1}^{2})\leq C(h^{6}+\|\theta\|^{2}+\|\xi\|_{1}^{2})\,,$ and, consequently, by Gronwall’s Lemma and $(\ref{eq53})$ that $\|\theta\|+\|\xi\|_{1}\leq Ch^{3}\,,\quad 0\leq t\leq T\,,$ and the conclusions of the Theorem follow in view of the approximation properties of $S_{h}^{2}$ and $S_{h,0}^{3}$ , Sobolev’s theorem and the $L^{\infty}$ - $L^{2}$ inverse inequality in $S_{h}^{2}$ .
∎ Remark 5.1 . In view of Theorems $1.3.1$ and $1.3.2$ of [ 30 ] the conclusions of Theorem $5.1$ remain valid if in place of $R_{h}u_{0}$ we use the elliptic projection of $u_{0}$ that is induced
by the bilinear form $a(\cdot,\cdot)$ as was done in previous sections. 5.2. Superaccuracy estimates for the error of the $L^{2}$ projection The proofs of Lemmas $5.1$ , $5.2$ , and $5.4$ depend on some superaccuracy properties
of the error of the $L^{2}$ projection onto $S_{h}^{2}$ of a smooth function, in the case of
uniform mesh. Suppose that $\eta\in C^{3}$ , $u\in C_{0}^{2}$ , and let $P$ be the $L^{2}$ projection onto $S_{h}^{2}$ and $\rho=\eta-P\eta$ . To estimate $(u\rho,\phi_{i}^{\prime})$ , where $\{\phi_{i}\}$ is the standard basis of $S_{h}^{2}$ ,
it suffices to estimate the integrals $\int_{I_{i}}u\rho dx\,,$ where $I_{i}=(x_{i},x_{i+1})$ , $i=1,2,\dots,N$ . Putting $x_{i+1/2}=(x_{i}+x_{i+1})/2$ , we will have $\int_{I_{i}}u\rho dx=u(x_{i+1/2})\int_{I_{i}}\rho dx+u^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})\int_{I_{i}}(x-x_{i+1/2})\rho(x)dx+\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{I_{i}}(x-x_{i+1/2})^{2}\rho(x)u^{\prime\prime}(y_{i})dx\,,$ for some $y_{i}=y_{i}(x)\in I_{i}$ . Since $\|\rho\|_{\infty}=O(h^{2})$ , the third integral in the
right-hand side is of $O(h^{5})$ . Now, if $x\in I_{i}$ $\rho(x)=\rho(x_{i+1/2})+(x-x_{i+1/2})\rho^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+\tfrac{1}{2}(x-x_{i+1/2})^{2}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+\tfrac{1}{6}(x-x_{i+1/2})^{3}\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tau_{i})\,,$ for some $\tau_{i}=\tau_{i}(x)\in I_{i}$ . Hence $\int_{I_{i}}(x-x_{i+1/2})\rho(x)dx=\rho^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})\int_{I_{i}}(x-x_{i+1/2})^{2}dx+O(h^{5})=\tfrac{h^{3}}{12}\rho^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+O(h^{5})\,.$ Thus $\int_{I_{i}}u\rho dx=u(x_{i+1/2})\int_{I_{i}}\rho dx+\tfrac{h^{3}}{12}\rho^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})u^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+O(h^{5})\,,$ and, consequently, we must estimate the integral of $\rho$ on $I_{i}$ and the derivative of $\rho$ at the midpoint of $I_{i}$ . Both of these quantities turn out to be superaccurate as
we prove by elementary techniques in the following two lemmas: Lemma 5.5 . Let $\eta\in C^{3}$ . Then $\max_{1\leq i\leq N}|\rho^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})|\leq Ch^{2}\,.$ Proof. For $x\in I_{i}$ we have $\rho^{\prime}(x)=\eta^{\prime}(x)-(P\eta)^{\prime}(x)=\eta^{\prime}(x)-\dfrac{c_{i+1}-c_{i}}{h}\,,$ i.e. $\rho^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})=\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})-\dfrac{c_{i+1}-c_{i}}{h}\,,\quad 1\leq i\leq N\,,$ (5.8) where $c_{i}$ is the $i$ th component of the solution $c$ of the linear system $Gc=b$ ,
where $G_{ij}=(\phi_{j},\phi_{i})$ , $1\leq i,j\leq N+1$ and $b_{i}=(\eta,\phi_{i})$ , $i=1,2,\dots,N+1$ . The equations of the system may be written explicitly as $\displaystyle 2c_{1}+c_{2}$ $\displaystyle=6b_{1}/h\,,$ (5.9) $\displaystyle c_{1}+4c_{2}+c_{3}$ $\displaystyle=6b_{2}/h\,,$ $\displaystyle c_{i-1}+4c_{i}+c_{i+1}$ $\displaystyle=6b_{i}/h\,,\quad i=3,4,\dots,N-1\,,$ $\displaystyle c_{N-1}+4c_{N}+c_{N+1}$ $\displaystyle=6b_{N}/h\,,$ $\displaystyle c_{N}+2c_{N+1}$ $\displaystyle=6b_{N+1}/h\,.$ Hence $\displaystyle 3(c_{2}-c_{1})+(c_{3}-c_{2})$ $\displaystyle=6(b_{2}-2b_{1})/h\,,$ $\displaystyle(c_{i}-c_{i-1})+4(c_{i+1}-c_{i})$ $\displaystyle+(c_{i+2}-c_{i+1})=6(b_{i+1}-b_{i})/h\,,\quad 2\leq i\leq N-1$ and $(c_{N}-c_{N-1})+3(c_{N+1}-c_{N})=6(2b_{N+1}-b_{N})/h\,.$ Therefore we obtain the following equations for the differences $(c_{i+1}-c_{i})/h$ : $\displaystyle 3(c_{2}-c_{1})/h+(c_{3}-c_{2})/h$ $\displaystyle=6(b_{2}-2b_{1})/h^{2}\,,$ $\displaystyle(c_{i}-c_{i-1})/h+4(c_{i+1}-c_{i})/h+(c_{i+2}-c_{i+1})/h$ $\displaystyle=6(b_{i+1}-b_{i})/h^{2}\,,\quad i=2,3,\dots,N-1\,,$ $\displaystyle(c_{N}-c_{N-1})/h+3(c_{N+1}-c_{N})/h$ $\displaystyle=6(2b_{N+1}-b_{N})/h^{2}\,.$ Hence, if we put $\varepsilon^{\prime}_{i}:=\rho^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})$ , $i=1,2,\dots,N$ , then $(\ref{eq58})$ implies that $\varepsilon^{\prime}=(\varepsilon_{i}^{\prime})$ is the solution of the linear system $A\varepsilon^{\prime}=r^{\prime}\,,$ (5.10) where $A$ is the $N\times N$ tridiagonal matrix with $A_{11}=A_{NN}=3$ , $A_{ii}=4$ , $2\leq i\leq N-1$ , and $A_{ij}=1$ if $|i-j|=1$ , and $r^{\prime}=(r^{\prime}_{1},r^{\prime}_{2},\dots,r^{\prime}_{N})^{T}$ ,
where $\displaystyle r^{\prime}_{1}$ $\displaystyle=3\eta^{\prime}(x_{3/2})+\eta^{\prime}(x_{5/2})-6(b_{2}-2b_{1})/h^{2}\,,$ (5.11) $\displaystyle r^{\prime}_{i}$ $\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}(x_{i-1/2})+4\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+3/2})-6(b_{i+1}-b_{i})/h^{2}\,,\quad i=2,3,\dots,N-1\,,$ $\displaystyle r^{\prime}_{N}$ $\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}(x_{N-1/2})+3\eta^{\prime}(x_{N+1/2})-6(2b_{N+1}-b_{N})/h^{2}\,.$ We will now show that there exists a constant $C$ depending only on $\|\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}$ , such that $\max_{1\leq i\leq N}|r^{\prime}_{i}|\leq Ch^{2}\,.$ (5.12) To estimate $r^{\prime}_{1}$ note that $\displaystyle b_{2}-2b_{1}=\int_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}\eta\phi_{2}dx-2\int_{I_{1}}\eta\phi_{1}dx$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{3}{h}\int_{I_{1}}(x-h)\eta dx-\tfrac{1}{h}\int_{I_{2}}(x-h)\eta dx+\int_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}\eta dx$ $\displaystyle=:\tfrac{3}{h}J_{1}-\tfrac{1}{h}J_{2}+J_{3}\,.$ Using Taylor’s theorem we have $\displaystyle J_{1}$ $\displaystyle=\int_{I_{1}}(x-h)\eta dx=-\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta(h)+\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}\eta^{\prime}(h)-\tfrac{h^{4}}{8}\eta^{\prime\prime}(h)+O(h^{5})\,,$ $\displaystyle J_{2}$ $\displaystyle=\int_{I_{2}}(x-h)\eta dx=\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta(h)+\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}\eta^{\prime}(h)+\tfrac{h^{4}}{8}\eta^{\prime\prime}(h)+O(h^{5})\,,$ $\displaystyle J_{3}$ $\displaystyle=\int_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}\eta dx=2h\eta(h)+\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}\eta^{\prime\prime}(h)+O(h^{4})\,,$ and thus $b_{2}-2b_{1}=\tfrac{2h^{2}}{3}\eta^{\prime}(h)-\tfrac{h^{3}}{6}\eta^{\prime\prime}(h)+O(h^{4})\,.$ In addition, $3\eta^{\prime}(x_{3/2})+\eta^{\prime}(x_{5/2})=4\eta^{\prime}(h)-h\eta^{\prime\prime}(h)+O(h^{2})\,.$ Therefore, by $(\ref{eq511})$ , we see that $r^{\prime}_{1}=O(h^{2})$ .
For $r^{\prime}_{N}$ we have $\displaystyle 2b_{N+1}-b_{N}$ $\displaystyle=2\int_{I_{N}}\eta\phi_{N+1}dx-\int_{I_{N-1}\cup I_{N}}\eta\phi_{N}dx$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{3}{h}\int_{I_{N}}(x-x_{N})\eta dx-\tfrac{1}{h}\int_{I_{N-1}}(x-x_{N})\eta dx-\int_{I_{N-1}\cup I_{N}}\eta dx$ $\displaystyle=:\tfrac{3}{h}J_{1}-\tfrac{1}{h}J_{2}-J_{3}\,.$ By Taylor’s theorem $\displaystyle J_{1}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}\eta^{\prime}(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{4}}{8}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{N})+O(h^{5})\,,$ $\displaystyle J_{2}$ $\displaystyle=-\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}\eta^{\prime}(x_{N})-\tfrac{h^{4}}{8}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{N})+O(h^{5})\,,$ $\displaystyle J_{3}$ $\displaystyle=2h\eta(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{N})+O(h^{4})\,,$ and thus $2b_{N+1}-b_{N}=\tfrac{2h^{2}}{3}\eta^{\prime}(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{6}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{N})+O(h^{4})\,.$ Hence,by $(\ref{eq511})$ $\displaystyle r^{\prime}_{N}$ $\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}(x_{N-1/2})+3\eta^{\prime}(x_{N+1/2})-\tfrac{6}{h^{2}}(2b_{N+1}-b_{N})$ $\displaystyle=4\eta^{\prime}(x_{N})+h\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{N})-4\eta^{\prime}(x_{N})-h\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{N})+O(h^{2})=O(h^{2})\,.$ For $r^{\prime}_{i}$ , $2\leq i\leq N-1$ , we have $\displaystyle b_{i+1}-b_{i}$ $\displaystyle=\int_{I_{i}\cup I_{i+1}}\eta\phi_{i+1}dx-\int_{I_{i-1}\cup I_{i}}\eta\phi_{i}dx$ (5.13) $\displaystyle=\tfrac{2}{h}\int_{I_{i}}(x-x_{i+1/2})\eta dx-\tfrac{1}{h}\int_{I_{i+1}}(x-x_{i+3/2})\eta dx-\tfrac{1}{h}\int_{I_{i-1}}(x-x_{i-1/2})\eta dx$ $\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{I_{i+1}}\eta dx-\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{I_{i-1}}\eta dx=:\tfrac{2}{h}J_{1}-\tfrac{1}{h}J_{2}-\tfrac{1}{h}J_{3}+\tfrac{1}{2}J_{4}-\tfrac{1}{2}J_{5}\,.$ By Taylor’s theorem we obtain $J_{1}=\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})\int_{I_{i}}(x-x_{i+1/2})^{2}dx+O(h^{5})=\tfrac{h^{3}}{12}\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+O(h^{5})\,.$ (5.14) Similarly $\displaystyle J_{2}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{h^{3}}{12}\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+3/2})+O(h^{5})\,,$ (5.15) $\displaystyle J_{3}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{h^{3}}{12}\eta^{\prime}(x_{i-1/2})+O(h^{5})\,,$ (5.16) $\displaystyle J_{4}$ $\displaystyle=h\eta(x_{i+3/2})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{24}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i+3/2})+O(h^{4})\,,$ $\displaystyle J_{5}$ $\displaystyle=h\eta(x_{i-1/2})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{24}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i-1/2})+O(h^{4})\,.$ Since now $J_{4}-J_{5}=2h^{2}\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+O(h^{4})\,.$ (5.17) Substituting $(\ref{eq514})$ - $(\ref{eq517})$ into $(\ref{eq513})$ we obtain $b_{i+1}-b_{i}=\tfrac{7h^{2}}{6}\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})-\tfrac{h^{2}}{12}\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+3/2})-\tfrac{h^{2}}{12}\eta^{\prime}(x_{i-1/2})+O(h^{4})\,,$ and finally, by $(\ref{eq511})$ $\displaystyle r^{\prime}_{i}$ $\displaystyle=\eta^{\prime}(x_{i-1/2})+4\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+3/2})-\tfrac{6}{h^{2}}(b_{i+1}-b_{i})$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{3}{2}(\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+3/2})-2\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+\eta^{\prime}(x_{i-1/2}))+O(h^{2})=O(h^{2})\,.$ We conclude therefore that $(\ref{eq512})$ holds with a constant $C=C(\|\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}\|_{\infty})$ .
Writing the linear system $(\ref{eq59})$ as $\tfrac{1}{4}A\varepsilon^{\prime}=\tfrac{1}{4}r^{\prime}$ with $\tfrac{1}{4}A=I-B$ , where $B$ is a $N\times N$ matrix with $\|B\|_{\infty}=1/2$ . Therefore, $\|(I-B)^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leq 2$ and consequently $\max_{1\leq i\leq N}|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{i}|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq N}|r^{\prime}_{i}|\leq Ch^{2}\,.$ ∎ Lemma 5.6 . Let $\eta\in C^{3}$ . Then for some constant $C=C(\|\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}\|_{\infty})$ we have $\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\bigl|\int_{I_{i}}\rho dx\bigr|\leq Ch^{4}\,.$ Proof. We have $\int_{I_{i}}\rho dx=\int_{I_{i}}\eta dx-\tfrac{h}{2}(c_{i+1}+c_{i})\,,\quad i=1,2,\dots,N\,,$ where $c_{i}$ is the $i$ -th component of the solution $c$ of the linear system $Gc=b$ with $G$ and $b$ as in the previous Lemma. From the equation $(\ref{eq59})$ we see that $\displaystyle 5\cdot\tfrac{h}{2}(c_{1}+c_{2})+\tfrac{h}{2}(c_{2}+c_{3})$ $\displaystyle=3(2b_{1}+b_{2})\,,$ $\displaystyle\tfrac{h}{2}(c_{i-1}+c_{i})+4\cdot\tfrac{h}{2}(c_{i}+c_{i+1})+\tfrac{h}{2}(c_{i+1}+c_{i+2})$ $\displaystyle=3(b_{i+1}+b_{i})\,,\quad 2\leq i\leq N-1\,,$ $\displaystyle\tfrac{h}{2}(c_{N-1}+c_{N})+5\cdot\tfrac{h}{2}(c_{N}+c_{N+1})$ $\displaystyle=3(b_{N}+2b_{N+1})\,.$ Hence, if $\varepsilon_{i}:=\int_{I_{i}}\rho dx\,,\quad i=1,2,\dots,N\,,$ then $\varepsilon=(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\dots,\varepsilon_{N})^{T}$ is the solution of the system $\Gamma\varepsilon=r\,,$ (5.18) where $\Gamma$ is the tridiagonal $N\times N$ matrix with $\Gamma_{11}=\Gamma_{NN}=5$ , $\Gamma_{ii}=4$ , $i=2,3,\dots,N-1$ , and $\Gamma_{ij}=1$ , when $|i-j|=1$ , and where $r=(r_{1},r_{2},\dots,r_{N})^{T}$ with $\displaystyle r_{1}$ $\displaystyle=5\int_{I_{1}}\eta dx+\int_{I_{2}}\eta dx-3(2b_{1}+b_{2})\,,$ (5.19) $\displaystyle r_{i}$ $\displaystyle=\int_{I_{i-1}}\eta dx+4\int_{I_{i}}\eta dx+\int_{I_{i+1}}\eta dx-3(b_{i+1}+b_{i})\,,\quad i=2,3,\dots,N-1\,,$ $\displaystyle r_{N}$ $\displaystyle=\int_{I_{N-1}}\eta dx+5\int_{I_{N}}\eta dx-3(b_{N}+2b_{N+1})\,.$ We will show that $r_{i}=O(h^{4})$ , for $i=1,2,\dots,N$ . For $r_{1}$ , note that $2b_{1}+b_{2}=2\int_{I_{1}}\eta\phi_{1}dx+\int_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}\eta\phi_{2}dx=\int_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}\eta dx-\tfrac{1}{h}\int_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}(x-h)\eta dx\,.$ Hence, by Taylor’s theorem $2b_{1}+b_{2}=2h\eta(h)-\tfrac{2h^{2}}{3}\eta^{\prime}(h)+\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}\eta^{\prime\prime}(h)+O(h^{4})\,.$ (5.20) Also $\displaystyle\int_{I_{1}}\eta dx$ $\displaystyle=h\eta(h)-\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta^{\prime}(h)+\tfrac{h^{3}}{6}\eta^{\prime\prime}(h)+O(h^{4})\,,$ $\displaystyle\int_{I_{2}}\eta dx$ $\displaystyle=h\eta(h)+\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta^{\prime}(h)+\tfrac{h^{3}}{6}\eta^{\prime\prime}(h)+O(h^{4})\,.$ From these relations and $(\ref{eq519})$ and $(\ref{eq520})$ we obtain $\displaystyle r_{1}$ $\displaystyle=5\bigl(h\eta(h)-\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta^{\prime}(h)+\tfrac{h^{3}}{6}\eta^{\prime\prime}(h)\bigr)+\bigl(h\eta(h)+\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta^{\prime}(h)+\tfrac{h^{3}}{6}\eta^{\prime\prime}(h)\bigr)$ $\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,-3\bigl(2h\eta(h)-\tfrac{2h^{2}}{3}\eta^{\prime}(h)+\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}\eta^{\prime\prime}(h)\bigr)+O(h^{4})=O(h^{4})\,.$ For $r_{N}$ we observe $b_{N}+2b_{N+1}=\int_{I_{N-1}\cup I_{N}}\eta\phi_{N}dx+2\int_{I_{N}}\eta\phi_{N+1}dx=\tfrac{1}{h}\int_{I_{N-1}\cup I_{N}}(x-x_{N})\eta dx+\int_{I_{N-1}\cup I_{N}}\eta dx\,.$ Hence, by Taylor’s theorem $b_{N}+2b_{N+1}=2h\eta(x_{N})+\tfrac{2h^{2}}{3}\eta^{\prime}(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{N})+O(h^{4})\,.$ (5.21) Also $\displaystyle\int_{I_{N-1}}\eta dx$ $\displaystyle=h\eta(x_{N})-\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta^{\prime}(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{6}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{N})+O(h^{4})\,,$ $\displaystyle\int_{I_{N}}\eta dx$ $\displaystyle=h\eta(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta^{\prime}(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{6}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{N})+O(h^{4})\,.$ From these relations and $(\ref{eq519})$ and $(\ref{eq521})$ we see that $\displaystyle r_{N}$ $\displaystyle=\bigl(h\eta(x_{N})-\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta^{\prime}(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{6}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{N})\bigr)+5\bigl(h\eta(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta^{\prime}(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{6}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{N})\bigr)$ $\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,-3\bigl(2h\eta(x_{N})+\tfrac{2h^{2}}{3}\eta^{\prime}(x_{N})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{N})\bigr)+O(h^{4})=O(h^{4})\,.$ For $r_{i}$ , $i=2,3,\dots,N-1$ , we have $\displaystyle b_{i}+b_{i+1}$ $\displaystyle=\int_{I_{i-1}\cup I_{i}}\eta\phi_{i}dx+\int_{I_{i}\cup I_{i+1}}\eta\phi_{i}dx$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{h}\int_{I_{i-1}}(x-x_{i-1})\eta dx+\int_{I_{i}}\eta dx+\tfrac{1}{h}\int_{I_{i+1}}(x_{i+2}-x)\eta dx\,.$ Thus, by $(\ref{eq519})$ we have, for $2\leq i\leq N-1$ $r_{i}=\int_{I_{i-1}\cup I_{i}\cup I_{i+1}}\eta dx-\tfrac{3}{h}\int_{I_{i-1}}(x-x_{i-1})\eta dx-\tfrac{3}{h}\int_{I_{i+1}}(x_{i+2}-x)\eta dx=:J_{1}-\tfrac{3}{h}J_{2}-\tfrac{3}{h}J_{3}\,.$ (5.22) By Taylor’s theorem $\displaystyle J_{2}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta(x_{i-1})+\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}\eta^{\prime}(x_{i-1})+\tfrac{h^{4}}{8}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i-1})+O(h^{5})\,,$ $\displaystyle J_{3}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}\eta(x_{i+2})-\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+2})+\tfrac{h^{4}}{8}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i+2})+O(h^{5})\,,$ i.e. $\displaystyle J_{2}+J_{3}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{h^{2}}{2}(\eta(x_{i-1})+\eta(x_{i+2}))+\tfrac{h^{3}}{3}(\eta^{\prime}(x_{i-1})-\eta^{\prime}(x_{i+2}))+\tfrac{h^{4}}{8}(\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i-1})+\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i+2}))+O(h^{5})$ $\displaystyle=h^{2}\eta(x_{i+1/2})+\tfrac{3h^{4}}{8}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+O(h^{5})\,.$ In addition, $J_{1}=3h\eta(x_{i+1/2})+\tfrac{1}{3}\tfrac{27h^{3}}{8}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+O(h^{4})\,.$ Substituting in $(\ref{eq522})$ and in $(\ref{eq519})$ we see that for $2\leq i\leq N-1$ $r_{i}=3h\eta(x_{i+1/2})+\tfrac{9h^{3}}{8}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i+1/2})-3h\eta(x_{i+1/2})-\tfrac{9h^{3}}{8}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+O(h^{4})=O(h^{4})\,.$ Hence, there exists a constant $C$ depending only on $\|\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}$ such that $\max_{1\leq i\leq N}|r_{i}|\leq Ch^{4}\,.$ (5.23) From $(\ref{eq518})$ we obtain that $\tfrac{1}{4}\Gamma\varepsilon=\tfrac{1}{4}r$ , where $\tfrac{1}{4}\Gamma=I-E$ , and $E$ is a $N\times N$ matrix with $\|E\|_{\infty}=1/2$ . Hence $\|(I-E)^{-1}\|_{\infty}\leq 2$ and thus $\max_{1\leq i\leq N}|\varepsilon_{i}|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq N}|r_{i}|\leq Ch^{4}\,.$ ∎ If we now assume that $\eta$ is smoother, then, using a result by Demko, [ 17 ] , (see also
de Boor, [ 16 ] ), we may show that $\int_{I_{i}}\rho dx=O(h^{5})$ provided that the endpoints $x_{i}$ , $x_{i+1}$ of the interval $I_{i}$ are at a distance of $O(h\ln 1/h)$ from the boundary
of $[0,1]$ . Lemma 5.7 . Let $\eta\in C^{4}$ . Then, there exists a constant $\widetilde{C}$ such that if $dist(x_{i},0)\geq\widetilde{C}h\ln 1/h\,\quad\text{and}\quad dist(x_{i+1},1)\geq\widetilde{C}h\ln 1/h\,,$ then $\int_{I_{i}}\rho dx=O(h^{5})\,.$ Proof. We will first show that due to our hypothesis of increased smoothness of $\eta$ , we now have $r_{i}=O(h^{5})\,,\quad i=2,3,\dots,N-1\,,$ where the $r_{i}$ were defined in $(\ref{eq519})$ and written in the form $(\ref{eq522})$ . Since $\eta\in C^{4}$ , using Simpson’s rule we approximate the integrals in $(\ref{eq522})$ by $\displaystyle\int_{I_{i-1}\cup I_{i}\cup I_{i+1}}\eta dx$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{3h}{6}\bigl(\eta(x_{i-1})+4\eta(x_{i+1/2})+\eta(x_{i+2})\bigr)+O(h^{5})\,,$ $\displaystyle\int_{I_{i-1}}\tfrac{x-x_{i-1}}{h}\eta dx$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{h}{6}\bigl(2\eta(x_{i-1/2})+\eta(x_{i})\bigr)+O(h^{5})\,,$ $\displaystyle\int_{I_{i+1}}\tfrac{x_{i+2}-x}{h}\eta dx$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{h}{6}\bigl(\eta(x_{i+1})+2\eta(x_{i+3/2})\bigr)+O(h^{5})\,,$ yielding $r_{i}=\tfrac{h}{2}\bigl(\eta(x_{i-1})-2\eta(x_{i-1/2})-\eta(x_{i})+4\eta(x_{i+1/2})+\eta(x_{i+2})-2\eta(x_{i+3/2})-\eta(x_{i+1})\bigr)+O(h^{5})\,.$ Hence, using Taylor’s theorem $\displaystyle r_{i}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{h}{2}\bigl(\tfrac{h^{2}}{4}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i-1/2})-2\tfrac{h^{2}}{4}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+\tfrac{h^{2}}{4}\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i+3/2})\bigr)+O(h^{5})$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{h^{3}}{8}\bigl(\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i-1/2})-2\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+\eta^{\prime\prime}(x_{i+3/2})\bigr)+O(h^{5})=O(h^{5})\,.$ We conclude that for constants $C_{1}$ , $C_{2}$ independent of $h$ we have, in view
of $(\ref{eq523})$ , that $|r_{1}|\leq C_{1}h^{4}\,,\quad\max_{2\leq i\leq N-1}|r_{i}|\leq C_{2}h^{5}\,,\quad|r_{N}|\leq C_{1}h^{4}\,.$ (5.24) We also recall from Lemma $5.6$ , cf. $(\ref{eq518})$ , that the $\varepsilon_{i}:=\int_{I_{i}}\rho dx$ , $1\leq i\leq N$ , form the solution of the system $\tfrac{1}{4}\Gamma\varepsilon=(I-E)\varepsilon=\tfrac{1}{4}r\,,$ where $\Gamma$ was defined after $(\ref{eq518})$ . Hence $\varepsilon_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\Gamma^{-1})_{ij}r_{j}=(\Gamma^{-1})_{i1}r_{1}+\sum_{j=2}^{N-1}(\Gamma^{-1})_{ij}r_{j}+(\Gamma^{-1})_{iN}r_{N}\,.$ (5.25) Now, using Proposition $2.1$ of [ 17 ] , we see that there is a constant $C_{3}$ such that $|(\Gamma^{-1})_{i1}|\leq C_{3}2^{-|i-1|}\,,\quad\text{and}\quad|(\Gamma^{-1})_{iN}|\leq C_{3}2^{-|i-N|}\,.$ (5.26) Therefore, if $dist(x_{i},0)\geq\tfrac{1}{\ln 2}h\ln 1/h$ and $dist(x_{i+1},1)\geq\tfrac{1}{\ln 2}h\ln 1/h$ , there follows that $i-1\geq\tfrac{1}{\ln 2}\ln 1/h\quad\text{and}\quad N-i\geq\tfrac{1}{\ln 2}\ln 1/h\,,$ i.e. $2^{-|i-1|}\leq h\quad\text{and}\quad 2^{-|i-N|}\leq h\,,$ and by $(\ref{eq526})$ $|(\Gamma^{-1})_{i1}|\leq C_{3}h\quad\text{and}\quad|(\Gamma^{-1})_{iN}|\leq C_{3}h\,.$ Hence, for the indices $i$ for which $1+\tfrac{1}{\ln 2}\ln 1/h\leq i\leq N-\tfrac{1}{\ln 2}\ln 1/h\,,$ we will have from $(\ref{eq525})$ and $(\ref{eq524})$ that $\displaystyle|\varepsilon_{i}|$ $\displaystyle\leq C_{3}h|r_{1}|+\sum_{j=2}^{N-1}|(\Gamma^{-1})_{ij}||r_{i}|+C_{3}h|r_{N}|$ $\displaystyle\leq C_{3}C_{1}h^{5}+\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|(\Gamma^{-1})_{ij}|C_{2}h^{5}+C_{3}C_{1}h^{5}\leq Ch^{5}\,.$ ∎ We now prove, as consequences of these results, several superaccurate estimates on weighted integrals
of the error of the $L^{2}$ projection. Lemma 5.8 . Let $\eta\in C^{4}$ , $u\in C_{0}^{2}$ , and $\rho=\eta-P\eta$ . Then, $\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\bigl|\int_{I_{i}}u\rho dx\bigr|\leq Ch^{5}\ln 1/h\,.$ Proof. If $1\leq i\leq N$ we have for $t_{i}=t_{i}(x)\in I_{i}$ $\int_{I_{i}}u\rho dx=u(x_{i+1/2})\int_{I_{i}}\rho dx+u^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})\int_{I_{i}}(x-x_{i+1/2})\rho dx+\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{I_{i}}(x-x_{i+1/2})^{2}\rho u^{\prime\prime}(t_{i})dx\,.$ The third integral of the right-hand side is of $O(h^{5})$ . For the second integral, by
Taylor’s theorem we have $\int_{I_{i}}(x-x_{i+1/2})\rho dx=\tfrac{h^{3}}{12}\rho^{\prime}(x_{i+1/2})+O(h^{5})\,,$ and using Lemma $5.5$ , $\int_{I_{i}}(x-x_{i+1/2})\rho dx=O(h^{5})\,.$ We conclude that $\int_{I_{i}}u\rho dx=u(x_{i+1/2})\int_{I_{i}}\rho dx+O(h^{5})\,.$ (5.27) If now $i$ is such that $dist(x_{i},0)\geq\widetilde{C}h\ln 1/h\,,\quad\text{and}\quad dist(x_{i+1},1)\geq\widetilde{C}h\ln 1/h\,,$ where $\widetilde{C}$ is the constant in the statement of Lemma $5.7$ , then $\int_{I_{i}}u\rho dx=O(h^{5})\,.$ (5.28) If $dist(x_{i},0)\leq\widetilde{C}h\ln 1/h$ , we have $u(x_{i+1/2})=u(x_{i})+\tfrac{h}{2}u^{\prime}(\tau_{i})\,,\quad u(x_{i})=x_{i}u^{\prime}(y_{i})\,,$ for some $\tau_{i}\in(x_{i},x_{i+1/2})$ and $y_{i}\in(0,x_{i})$ . Therefore $|u(x_{i+1/2})|\leq Ch\ln 1/h\,,$ and from Lemma $5.6$ and $(\ref{eq527})$ we have for such $i$ that $\bigl|\int_{I_{i}}u\rho dx\bigr|\leq Ch^{5}\ln 1/h\,.$ (5.29) If finally $dist(x_{i+1},1)\leq\widetilde{C}h\ln 1/h$ , we have $u(x_{i+1/2})=u(x_{i+1})-\tfrac{h}{2}u^{\prime}(\widetilde{\tau}_{i})\,,\quad u(x_{i+1})=(1-x_{i+1})u^{\prime}(\widetilde{y}_{i})\,,$ for some $\widetilde{\tau}_{i}\in(x_{i+1/2},x_{i+1})$ and $\widetilde{y}_{i}\in(x_{i+1},1)$ .
Hence $|u(x_{i+1/2})|\leq Ch\ln 1/h\,,$ and it follows again from Lemma $5.6$ and $(\ref{eq527})$ that in this case too $\bigl|\int_{I_{i}}u\rho dx\bigr|\leq Ch^{5}\ln 1/h\,.$ (5.30) The conclusion of the Lemma follows from $(\ref{eq528})$ - $(\ref{eq530})$ .
∎ Lemma 5.9 . Let $\eta\in C^{4}$ , $u\in C_{0}^{2}$ , $\rho=\eta-P\eta$ , and $\beta_{i}:=(u\rho,\phi^{\prime}_{i})\,,\quad i=1,2,\dots,N+1\,.$ If $\beta=(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\dots,\beta_{N+1})^{T}$ then $|\beta|\leq Ch^{3.5}\,,$ where $|\cdot|$ is the $l_{2}$ norm in $\mathbb{R}^{N+1}$ . Proof. We have $\displaystyle\beta_{1}$ $\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{h}\int_{I_{1}}u\rho dx\,,$ $\displaystyle\beta_{i}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{h}\int_{I_{i-1}}u\rho dx-\tfrac{1}{h}\int_{I_{i}}u\rho dx\,,\quad i=2,3,\dots,N\,,$ $\displaystyle\beta_{N+1}$ $\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{h}\int_{I_{N}}u\rho dx\,.$ If $dist(x_{i-1},0)\geq\widetilde{C}h\ln 1/h$ and $dist(x_{i+1},1)\geq\widetilde{C}h\ln 1/h$ ,
i.e. if $i\in K$ , where $K$ are the integers in the interval $[2+\widetilde{C}\ln 1/h,N-\widetilde{C}\ln 1/h]$ , then by $(\ref{eq528})$ $|\beta_{i}|\leq Ch^{4}\,.$ (5.31) If on the other hand $dist(x_{i-1},0)\leq\widetilde{C}h\ln 1/h$ or $dist(x_{i+1},1)\leq\widetilde{C}h\ln 1/h$ , i.e. if $i\in K_{1}$ , where $K_{1}$ are the integers
in $[1,1+\widetilde{C}\ln 1/h]\cup[N+1-\widetilde{C}\ln 1/h,N+1]$ , then, by $(\ref{eq529})$ and $(\ref{eq530})$ $|\beta_{i}|\leq Ch^{4}\ln 1/h\,.$ Therefore, $\displaystyle|\beta|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{N+1}|\beta_{i}|^{2}$ $\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in K_{1}}|\beta_{i}|^{2}+\sum_{i\in K}|\beta_{i}|^{2}$ $\displaystyle\leq(1+2\widetilde{C}\ln 1/h)Ch^{8}(\ln 1/h)^{2}+C(N-1-2\widetilde{C}\ln 1/h)h^{8}\leq Ch^{7}\,.$ ∎ Lemma 5.10 . Let $w\in C^{2}$ , $v\in C^{1}$ and
êáé $\beta_{i}=(v(w-Pw),\phi_{i})\,,\quad i=1,2,\dots,N+1\,.$ If $\beta=(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\dots,\beta_{N+1})^{T}$ , then $|\beta|\leq Ch^{3.5}\,,$ where $|\cdot|$ is the $l_{2}$ norm in $\mathbb{R}^{N+1}$ . Proof. For $x\in I_{1}$ we have $v(x)=v(h)+O(h)\,,$ and since $\|w-Pw\|_{\infty}=O(h^{2})$ , $v(x)(w-Pw)(x)=v(h)(w-Pw)(x)+O(h^{3})\,,$ and therefore $\beta_{1}=O(h^{4})\,.$ If $x\in I_{i-1}\cup I_{i}$ , $i=2,3,\dots,N$ , we have $v(x)=v(x_{i})+O(h)\,,$ whence $v(x)(w-Pw)(x)=v(x_{i})(w-Pw)(x)+O(h^{3})\,,$ and so $\beta_{i}=O(h^{4})\,.$ Similarly, $\beta_{N+1}=O(h^{4})$ , and finally $|\beta|=O(h^{3.5})$ .

nonhomogeneous system with a suitable right-hand side so that the solution is $\eta=\exp(2t)(\cos(\pi x)+x^{2}+2)$ , $u=\exp(xt)(\sin(\pi x)+x^{3}-x^{2})$ .) We integrated
the semidiscrete system up to $T=1$ by the fourth-order accurate explicit Runge-Kutta method
analyzed in paragraph $4.3$ with time step $k=h/10$ ; the temporal discretization error was
